VectorCraft MCP server

← 返回文档归档菜单

VectorCraft MCP server

vectorcraft-cli mcp runs a Model Context Protocol server on stdio (newline-delimited JSON-RPC 2.0; see Protocol for version negotiation). Agents use it to draw, inspect and look at VectorCraft documents.

It has two backends:

ModeHowWhat works
Remotevectorcraft-cli mcp --connect 127.0.0.1:7979 (the app must run with vectorcraft --control 7979)Everything. Tool calls are forwarded over the control protocol, so you watch the app change live.
Headlessvectorcraft-cli mcp --headlessAn in-process engine session with a CPU renderer. Everything except the UI-only tools (inspect_ui or ui_inspect, type_text, answer_dialog, open_panel, screenshot {window:true} or ui_screenshot).

With no flag, the server tries 127.0.0.1:7979 and falls back to headless. Logs go to stderr; stdout carries only protocol messages.

Install and register

From the repository root, with Rust 1.95+ and the platform's source-build prerequisites installed:

cargo install --path apps/vectorcraft-cli --locked
# POSIX shells, for Cargo's default install location:
export PATH="${CARGO_HOME:-$HOME/.cargo}/bin:$PATH"
vectorcraft-cli --help
claude mcp add vectorcraft -- vectorcraft-cli mcp
# or pin a mode:
claude mcp add vectorcraft-headless -- vectorcraft-cli mcp --headless
claude mcp add vectorcraft-app -- vectorcraft-cli mcp --connect 127.0.0.1:7979

Cargo installs a binary, not a symlink into target/, so it survives cargo clean. Re-run the install command after updating the checkout. By default the executable goes in $CARGO_HOME/bin (~/.cargo/bin when unset), or %USERPROFILE%\.cargo\bin on Windows. If you set --root, CARGO_INSTALL_ROOT, or Cargo's install.root, use that installation root's bin directory instead. Add it to the PATH inherited by the MCP client, not just an interactive shell, and restart the client after changing its environment. Installing does not start or register a server.

Other clients use the same command in their JSON config:

{"mcpServers": {"vectorcraft": {"command": "vectorcraft-cli", "args": ["mcp"]}}}

If a client reports ENOENT or "Executable not found in $PATH", verify the install with command -v vectorcraft-cli (PowerShell: Get-Command vectorcraft-cli) in the client's environment. If that environment cannot be changed, set JSON command to the absolute installed binary path, not a literal ~ or $HOME expression. Avoid symlinks into target/release: cleaning the build directory breaks them. A source build alone does not put the CLI on PATH.

For a live session, start the app first: cargo run --release -p vectorcraft -- --control 7979.

From an installed release

The release packages ship vectorcraft-cli alongside the desktop app, so no build is needed:

InstallCLI
Windows (MSI)C:\Program Files\VectorCraft\vectorcraft-cli.exe by default (wherever you installed it otherwise), not on PATH
Linux (deb, rpm)/usr/bin/vectorcraft-cli
macOSthe separate vectorcraft-cli-<version>-macos-<arch>.zip release asset (the .app holds only the desktop app)
# Windows, default install folder
claude mcp add vectorcraft -- "C:\Program Files\VectorCraft\vectorcraft-cli.exe" mcp
# Linux, or macOS with the CLI unzipped onto PATH
claude mcp add vectorcraft -- vectorcraft-cli mcp

For a live session, start the installed app with the control port before the MCP client starts the server:

# macOS: quit VectorCraft first, since `open -a` passes no arguments to an app that is already running
open -a VectorCraft --args --control 7979
# Windows (PowerShell), default install folder
& "C:\Program Files\VectorCraft\vectorcraft.exe" --control 7979
# Linux (deb, rpm)
vectorcraft --control 7979

The Dock, Start menu and desktop launchers start the app without --control, so the app opens no control port unless VECTORCRAFT_CONTROL_PORT is set. vectorcraft-cli mcp with no flag tries 127.0.0.1:7979 once, when it starts: a server that started before the app stays headless until the MCP client restarts it. open -a and PowerShell's & return before the app opens the port, so wait until the VectorCraft window is open before you start the MCP client. With --connect, a server that finds no app stops and prints vectorcraft-cli: cannot connect to 127.0.0.1:7979: followed by the system's connection error, for example connection timed out.

Confining file access

vectorcraft-cli mcp --automation-read-root /work/project --automation-write-root /work/project/out
vectorcraft-cli mcp --automation-read-root /work/project --automation-read-root /work/shared-assets \
  --automation-write-root /work/project/out --automation-write-root /tmp/scratch

{"mcpServers": {"vectorcraft": {"command": "vectorcraft-cli",
  "args": ["mcp", "--automation-read-root", "/work/project", "--automation-write-root", "/work/project/out"]}}}

The two flags (PhotoCraft and the other Craft apps take the same ones, so one client config works across the suite) keep the agent to the folders you give it:

  • Every file a command reads must lie inside a read root: open_file, file.place, relinking and updating links, the images an SVG links to, swatch library, graphic style library, symbol library, brush library and colour-profile loads, plug-ins, file.newFromTemplate, preset imports, shortcut sets (shortcutSet.read), the folders text.findFontFiles and links.findFiles search, the scripts script.check reads and the files they include.
  • Every file a command writes must lie inside a write root: save_file, export, the save and export commands, Export for Screens, Package, Export for Web, print to file, screenshot {path}, library and preset saves to a path, the copies links.relink {copy} makes.
  • file.list {path} (through run_command) lists a folder's files and folders, held to the read roots like any read, so an agent can see what it was given (#1163).
  • Each flag may be given more than once (#3546): a path inside any of the read roots may be read, one inside any of the write roots written, so one session can read a project folder and a shared assets folder, or write to an output folder and a scratch folder, without widening a root to their common parent. A folder given twice counts once.
  • Read and write access are separate. Give the same folder to both flags for both; a kind left out grants none of its access (with only --automation-read-root, nothing can be written to disk, though export and the save commands without a path still return the bytes). Without either flag nothing changes.
  • A path is made absolute against the server's working directory, then its links are followed: a file (or folder) link that leads out of a root is outside it. For a file that doesn't exist yet, its nearest existing folder's links are followed and the rest must be plain names, so .. below a folder that doesn't exist is refused, as is a link that leads nowhere (writing through it would create its target). The result is compared with the root by whole folder names: /work2/x is not inside /work. On Windows case is ignored, UNC paths (\\server\share\…) are resolved like drive-letter ones, and device names (CON, NUL, COM1…) are refused.
  • With --connect, the server makes the paths of open_file, save_file, export (path or options.path) and screenshot {path} absolute against its own working directory before it sends them to the app. Paths inside run_command and command_batch params go to the app as given, and the app resolves them against its own working directory.
  • A refused path is an ordinary tool error the agent can read and correct, for example automation path rejected: outside the write root /work/project/out: /etc/x.png (with several, outside the write roots /work/project/out, /tmp/scratch: …) or automation filesystem access is not granted: write authority is absent: /work/project/out/x.png. Links in an opened document that point outside the read roots show as missing.
  • The preferences that move folders the app reads or writes on its own (fontsFolder, pluginsFolder, recoveryFolder, templatesFolder) can only be set inside the roots. The app's own folders (preferences, Data Recovery, the User Defined library folders, VectorCraft's Fonts folder) are not confined: no agent names a path there.
  • The roots must be existing folders; a missing one stops the server with an error. Each flag takes one folder per use (--flag <dir> or --flag=<dir>) and may be used again for more.

The flags confine the headless server and imply --headless; --connect with them is an error, since the server would otherwise talk to an app that isn't confined. A running app confines itself: start it with the same flags, `vectorcraft --control 7979 --automation-read-root

--automation-write-root

`, and connect as usual (see [Control protocol](control-protocol.md#confining-file-access)). The server then writes nothing itself: `screenshot {path}` asks the app to write the file.

The checks hold for every thread of the server process. A path is checked, then opened: another program that swaps a link in between can still win that race, so the roots keep an agent to the files it was given rather than guarding against other software on the computer.

Protocol

Newline-delimited JSON-RPC 2.0 on stdio. The revision is 2025-06-18; 2025-03-26 and 2024-11-05 are accepted too. Every request and notification must carry "jsonrpc": "2.0": anything else is refused with -32600 (requests) or ignored (notifications, which have no id to address a reply to) before it is dispatched. When a tool call reaches a running app, it is one control-channel request, and the app echoes its id in the reply: a reply with any other id is rejected, the connection dropped, and the next call starts a fresh one — the call reports uncertainty like any reply that never arrived whole. A malformed JSON reply also drops the connection and reports that uncertainty; the failed operation is never replayed automatically. The app's refusals carry no id (a connection past its 16, a line it would not read): the call reports the app's reason and says nothing was run. Before each call the server checks that the app still holds the connection and opens a new one when it doesn't (the app closes a connection left idle for 5 minutes, and a restarted app is a new one), so a request is never written to a closed socket; when the app can't be reached, the call says that nothing was sent. Clients requesting 2026-07-28 through initialize, or through a request's _meta["io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion"], receive resultType: "complete", ttlMs and cacheScope: "private" on tool/resource/template/prompt lists and resource/prompt reads. List TTL is 600000 ms; live reads use zero. Older clients keep the original response shapes. This does not add streaming or input requests — see Not implemented.

One message is one line of at most 16 MiB: a longer line is answered with -32600 and skipped, and the server keeps serving what follows. A batch holds at most 64 messages. command_batch holds at most 256 steps, and the step results it keeps (for later steps' "$N.path" references and the reply) total at most 8 MiB — past that the batch stops with the reason, closing its atomic undo group as usual. Replies from a connected app are refused past 256 MiB and the connection dropped; the call reports uncertainty like any reply that never arrived whole.

initialize advertises:

CapabilityWhat it covers
toolsThe tools below
resourcesThree fixed resources and four templates
promptsFive ready-made workflows
completionscompletion/complete for prompt arguments and template variables
logginglogging/setLevel and notifications/message

Prompts

prompts/list offers five workflows; prompts/get returns one templated user message with its arguments filled in. A missing required argument is -32602.

PromptArguments (required first)
posterbrief, palette, text
icon-setsubject, count, detail
recolorpalette, method
trace-and-stylepath, preset, effect
export-setformats, directory, scale
echo '{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"method":"prompts/get","params":{"name":"poster","arguments":{"brief":"a jazz festival"}}}' \
  | vectorcraft-cli mcp --headless

Resource templates

vectorcraft://document (summary), vectorcraft://document/json (the whole model), and vectorcraft://commands (the live command catalog with enabled state) are the fixed resources. The templates read one thing at a time, which matters on a real document: the full model is thousands of lines an agent pays for again on every change.

uriTemplateReads
vectorcraft://object/{id}One layer or object with its children, bounds and paint: document.node {id, summary: true}, the node as document.inspect lists it. A large container can be sliced with run_command document.node {id, summary: true, depth?, childLimit?} (a level that shows fewer children reports childCount)
vectorcraft://command/{id}One command: label, menu path, shortcut, parameter description, enablement
vectorcraft://effect/{id}One live effect with its parameters and defaults
vectorcraft://swatch/{name}One swatch, colour, gradient or pattern swatch (percent-encode spaces)

An unknown URI is -32002; a template with no value, or a value that names nothing, is -32602.

The same reads work as query commands through run_command: document.node {id} returns the object's full model JSON (geometry, appearance, every attribute) and document.node {id, summary: true} the compact summary document.inspect gives for it (id, name, kind, bounds, paint labels, children), without summarizing the whole document. A summary read slices a large container: depth is how many child levels it includes (0: the node alone) and childLimit how many children each node shows, top of the stack first (both default to all). A level that shows fewer children than it has reports their total as childCount, so truncation is never silent; with nothing truncated the reply is the plain summary. Both must be non-negative integers and need summary: true (the full object JSON is never truncated); anything else is an error.

In the summary an object reports its paint as fill, stroke (labels) and strokeWidth. So does a compound path, whose paint is its own: its member paths report compoundMember: true instead of a paint they don't have. Every node (layers and groups too) reports its opacity when it is below 1, in both scales: opacity is the 0–1 fraction the document stores and opacityPct the 0–100 % that object.setProps, transparency.set, draw_path and draw_shape take (so an agent passes opacityPct back, never opacity); and blend when it isn't Normal, as the label object.setProps {blend} takes ("Multiply", "Color Burn").

On a large document, drill instead of dumping: inspect_document {depth: 0} (or run_command document.inspect {depth?, childLimit?}, same rules) is the skeleton (artboards with top layers and counts), run_command document.find {name?, kind?, text?, within?, intersects?, minArea?, …, select?, limit?} locates nodes across the whole tree, and document.node reads the interesting ones sliced. document.inspect and the doc_inspect / inspect_document tools also scope the reply themselves: ids: [id…] lists just those nodes (any level, in the order given, an unknown id is an error, at most 10000), kind lists every node of one panel label (ignoring case; within the subtrees of ids when both are given) with its ancestor path, and limit / offset page the list. A scoped reply carries the nodes as nodes instead of layers (each the summary the full tree gives it, sliced by depth / childLimit) plus total, the entries before paging; artboards, selection and history stay whole. limit / offset alone page the top layers (layers and total). Without these options the reply is the whole layer tree as before. document.find needs at least one filter and all given filters must match; matching ignores case, name is a substring of the Layers panel name, text a substring of type content and kind the exact panel label (Layer, Group, Clip Group, Path, Compound Path, Type, Image, Rectangle, ...). The geometric filters read each object's geometric bounds, in the document points document.inspect reports (groups included; layers, sublayers and a node without bounds do not match): within: [x, y, width, height] keeps objects inside that rectangle (edges included), intersects: [x, y, width, height] objects touching or overlapping it, minWidth / maxWidth and minHeight / maxHeight limit the bounds' size, minThickness / maxThickness the smaller of width and height (thin slivers of either orientation), and minArea / maxArea width × height. minAnchors / maxAnchors count a path's anchor points, a compound path's members together; other nodes do not match them. Limits are inclusive, not negative, and a minimum above its maximum is an error. select: true also selects the matches, leaving out hidden and locked ones, those inside another match, and a compound path's members unless the compound path is isolated; Select › Reselect repeats the find, and an action being recorded records it as a step (a find without select is not recorded). For example, document.find {maxThickness: 1, kind: "path", select: true} followed by edit.clear deletes hairline slivers. It answers {matches: [{id, name, kind, path}], total} top of the stack first, where path is the ancestor chain (layer first) and limit caps matches (default 100; 0 only counts); with select the reply adds selected, the number of objects selected. document.json stays whole: it is the fidelity path, not the way to look around.

Completions

completion/complete answers for a prompt argument (ref/prompt) or a template variable (ref/resource), ranked by prefix and then by substring, capped at 100 values with total and hasMore. The values are read from the live catalogues, so they follow the app: formats suggests what the engine writes, preset the Image Trace presets, effect the effect ids, {id} the layer and object ids in the document, {name} its swatch names. An unknown reference comes back empty rather than as an error, since a completion is asked for mid-typing.

Logging

logging/setLevel accepts any RFC 5424 severity — debug, info, notice, warning, error, critical, alert, emergency — and an unknown one is -32602 with the previous level left in place. From then on the engine's own log records arrive as notifications/message with level, logger and data. A null level turns it back off. Nothing is sent before the client asks, and records queued at the old level are dropped rather than replayed at the new one.

Installing the logger that receives those records is vectorcraft-cli's job, not the library's: a crate that installed one would override whatever logger an embedder had already set up. Embedding vectorcraft-mcp directly means calling vectorcraft_mcp::logging::install() yourself if you want log records to reach your client.

Long calls: cancellation, progress and a busy engine

A call runs to the end before its reply, and the engine runs one call at a time, in the order the lines arrived. A thread of its own reads the stream meanwhile, so the server still hears the client while an export runs (#2165, #2171, #2172):

  • Cancellation. notifications/cancelled {requestId} (spec) takes a request out of the queue, or stops the one that is running, and that request gets no reply. A running export, flatten, bake or render notices at its next checkpoint (between pages and artboards, between flattened groups, while drawing objects), returns, and writes nothing: the document is exactly as it was and the engine is free for the next call. An unknown, finished or malformed requestId is ignored, and so is a cancel of initialize. A call without checkpoints (every ordinary edit is over in milliseconds) runs to its end, but its reply is dropped all the same. Against a running app (--connect) the server stops waiting and drops the connection, but the app cannot be told to stop what it started.
  • Progress. A request with _meta.progressToken gets notifications/progress (spec) before its reply: the PDF writer reports "Writing page 2 of 5" with progress 2 and total 5, raster export reports each artboard, document.exportForScreens and assets.export report each file ("Exporting file 3 of 250", in place of their encoders' steps), and the PDF flattening stage reports a message without a total. progress only grows, updates come at most every 200 ms (the last page always), and none follows the reply. A client that does not read fast enough misses updates, never the reply.
  • A busy engine. ping, tools/list, resources/list, resources/templates/list, prompts/list and prompts/get need no engine and are answered at once, whatever is running. Anything else that arrives while a call has already run for two seconds is not queued behind it: it is answered with error -32003 (data.busy names the running call, its request id and how long it has run) and was not run, so the client can retry later or cancel the running call. Requests that arrive sooner than that queue and are answered in order, as before, so a client that sends a few quick calls together sees no difference. At most 64 lines wait; past that the server stops reading until the engine catches up.

vectorcraft_mcp::Server::serve takes the input and the output on separate threads (Send), and Server::with_busy_after changes the two seconds. Calling handle_line directly is as before: one line in, one reply out, no threads.

Not implemented

  • resources/subscribe / notifications/resources/updated. It would also have to be honest about whose edits it sees: comparing the undo history's length misses edits made in the app window, which is the main reason to run against a live document. document.inspect's revision is the right signal — it is monotonic and already bumped when a document is replaced — but noticing an edit still means someone asking.
  • Reading the document during a long call. Requests that need the engine (doc_inspect, resources/read, command_list) are refused as busy while an export runs, not answered from a snapshot.
  • sampling/createMessage and elicitation/create. Both are server→client requests. Supporting them means being able to suspend a tool call mid-flight, which is what the 2026-07-28 revision's input-requests mechanism exists for.
  • Streamable HTTP / SSE. stdio only.

completion/complete covers the "what could I pass here" half of the same problem, which is why it is here and subscriptions are not.

Tools

Common command tools

These names match the conventions in FilmCraft #28. The older names below remain listed because existing workflows use them.

ToolArguments and result
command_listOptional filter (or query), menu (a menu path substring, levels joined with >), enabled_only, brief (no params text); returns the command array. list_commands lists the same commands; it takes enabledOnly and returns {count, commands}
command_runid, optional params; runs the command through the existing backend. An unknown key in params is an error that names it and the nearest key the command takes (invalid parameters for file.new: unknown parameter widht; did you mean width?), so a misspelt key no longer runs the command with its default (see Parameter keys). run_command runs a command the same way and takes its id as command
command_batchsteps: [{id, params?}] (at most 256), optional stop_on_error (default true), atomic and label; returns completed, failed, results: [{ok, result|error}] and created (the ids the draw_path / draw_shape steps made, in order). A step's id is a command id, or draw_path / draw_shape with that tool's arguments as params (checked as tools/call checks them; the step's result is the tool's, so "$N.id" is the new object). Each edit has its own undo step, or with atomic: true the batch's edits are one undo step named label (else after its first edit; edit.undoGroup.begin/end underneath), and the edits made in a document that a step opens or creates (document.open, file.new) are one undo step there, named the same, also when no document was open before the batch. In a document that was open before the batch and that a step switches to (document.activate), each edit stays its own undo step; failures set isError. A param string "$N.path" or "$last.path" takes a value from an earlier step's result (see run below). Kept step results total at most 8 MiB; past that the batch stops with the reason. This is the MCP tool, not the engine command command.batch (that one takes commands: [{command, params}] and is what the Actions panel plays); use command_run with command.batch when replaying a recorded action
command_listOptional filter (or query), menu (a menu path substring, levels joined with >), enabled_only, brief (no params text); returns the command array
command_runid, optional params; runs the command through the existing backend. An unknown key in params is an error that names it and the nearest key the command takes (invalid parameters for file.new: unknown parameter widht; did you mean width?), so a misspelt key no longer runs the command with its default (see Parameter keys)
doc_inspectOptional depth, childLimit, ids, kind, limit, offset; same summary as inspect_document
render_previewOptional max_side (1–4096, default 1024), artboard (0-based, default 0) and traceViews; inline PNG of that artboard without editing it, drawn as export draws a PNG with background: "white" at the scale that fits max_side (at most 1). With traceViews: true, Image Trace objects draw in the view set with imageTrace.setView (outlines, the source image…), as in the app's window; by default they draw their tracing result. Artboards too large to render within the allocation bound return a tool error
ui_inspect, ui_screenshotConnected desktop state/window capture; tool errors in headless mode. ui_inspect is the same call as inspect_ui, and ui_screenshot is screenshot with window: true. ui_screenshot takes an optional max_side (1–4096), as screenshot {window: true} does: a capture whose longer side is larger is scaled down to max_side pixels on that side, and the reply's text gives the returned size as width/height and the capture's as capturedWidth/capturedHeight. Without max_side the full capture comes back

Every tool declares read-only, destructive, idempotent and open-world hints. File-writing screenshot, save_file and export are conservatively marked as mutations. Unknown top-level argument keys return JSON-RPC -32602; command failures and caught tool panics return isError content, and the server continues serving. Command params are passed through: the registry currently describes them in prose, so MCP does not guess schemas or silently remove keys.

Coordinates are points in document space: y points down, the origin is the first artboard's top-left, and a new document is 612 × 792 (US Letter). New objects become the selection. Most commands act on the selection or on explicit ids. A command that leaves out an object selected inside a selected group also leaves it out when ids list it together with a group that holds it. The commands that select their ids first (object.group, the object.arrange.* commands, object.move, the Pathfinder commands and the others like them) refuse an id that names a layer or sublayer, names no object or is not a non-negative integer, with an error that names the command that was run, and leave the document and the selection unchanged; layer.move reorders layers. explicit ids, which stand in for the selection, so they work with nothing selected. Where a command's params say ids?|id? (or id?, ids?: … (instead of id)), id is ids of one object: {"id": 7} does what {"ids": [7]} does, with or without a selection, and an id or ids that is malformed (not a non-negative integer) or names no object is refused under the command's name, never replaced by the selection (effect.apply {"id": "warp.arc"} without effect names the effect). A command that leaves out an object selected inside a selected group also leaves it out when ids list it together with a group that holds it.

Paint values (fill, stroke) accept "#rrggbb", "none", [r,g,b] (0..1), {"c","m","y","k"}, {"gray"}, or a full paint.setFill params object ({"gradient": …}, {"swatch": "name"}). For draw_shape and draw_path, omitted paint fields inherit the current new-art appearance. Both also style the new object in the same call: dash (lengths, or null for solid), cap (butt|round|square) and join (miter|round|bevel) go to stroke.set with strokeWidth; opacity (0–100 %) and name to object.setProps; effects: [{effect, params?}] are applied in order as apply_effect does, to the whole object. They are checked before anything is drawn (effect ids against effect.list), so a bad one draws nothing. The object and its styling are one command.batch, so the call is one undo step (named as the drawing command names its own, e.g. Pen or Rectangle) and a paint the engine refuses (a colour it can't read, a swatch the document lacks) fails the call and leaves the document as it was. The reply adds name, opacity and effects (each effect.apply result) when given; paint lists the stroke options applied. A gradient given to several objects fits each one's own bounds; paint.setFill / paint.setStroke with span: "selection" place a linear or radial gradient once on the union of the targets' bounds instead, so one ramp runs across them. For draw_shape and draw_path, omitted paint fields inherit the current new-art appearance. For a filled shape or path without an outline, pass an explicit fill and stroke: "none". Lines start with no fill and a visible stroke. The Fill/Stroke proxy commands run through run_command: paint.invert and paint.complement recolour the active proxy keeping each colour's model, paint.lastColor / paint.lastGradient re-apply the last solid colour or gradient, and paint.recent returns the recent colours that every paint command (and the eyedropper) feeds. paint.proxies returns what the proxies show: the fill and stroke, which one is active, and whether the selected objects' fills or strokes differ (fillMixed / strokeMixed, drawn as a "?" proxy).

ToolArgumentsNotes
list_commands{filter?, query?, menu?, enabledOnly?, brief?}The command catalogue: id, label, menu, shortcut, params doc (left out with brief), enablement.
run_command{command, params?}Runs any command. Use it for everything without a dedicated tool.
inspect_document{depth?, childLimit?, ids?, kind?, limit?, offset?}Artboards (with color, #rrggbb, for one with a background color), layer tree (ids, kinds, bounds, paint), selection, history, tool. A type node's fill, stroke and strokeWidth are the paint its characters show (its first run's); fills and strokes of the type object itself come as objectFill, objectStroke and objectStrokeWidth. Paint comes as a label ("#ff0000", "None", "G1 40% (#ff9999)" for a swatch at a tint); paint.proxies gives it as an object.
inspect_ui{}UI state. Remote mode only.
select_tool{tool}selection, directSelection, pen, rectangle, ellipse, polygon, star, lineSegment, …
pointer_gesture{events:[{kind,x,y,mods?}], tool?, mods?}kind is one of down, drag, up, move, doubleclick. Events go through the same path as the mouse. Headless, a move replies after the tool's highlight under the pointer is ready, including the Shape Builder's and the Live Paint Bucket's regions of a large selection, which the desktop app finds off the UI thread and highlights after the reply.
draw_path{points | d, closed?, fill?, stroke?, strokeWidth?, dash?, cap?, join?, opacity?, name?, effects?}points is [[x,y],…] or [{x,y,in?,out?,smooth?},…]. d is SVG path data. The options are below.
draw_shape{shape, …geometry, fill?, stroke?, strokeWidth?, dash?, cap?, join?, opacity?, name?, effects?}rectangle/ellipse: x,y,width,height (plus radius for corners). polygon: cx,cy,radius,sides. star: cx,cy,radius1,radius2,points. line: x1,y1,x2,y2.
set_paint{fill?, stroke?, strokeWidth?, ids?}Applies to the selection (or ids) and becomes the default for new art. Returns {applied, objects}: objects is the number of objects painted, as the objects of the paint.setFill, paint.setStroke and stroke.set replies (the largest of them): a selected group counts the objects inside it. When it is 0, a note says that only the paint for new art changed.
press_key{key, mods?}Remote: a real key event. Headless: runs the command or tool bound to that shortcut, or sends the key to the busy tool (digits too: 5 while dragging with the Perspective Selection tool; e while dragging a Selection or Group Selection marquee).
type_text{text}Remote only.
answer_dialog{set?: [{field, value?}], action?}Remote only. Sets fields of the app's open dialog in the order given with one ui.dialog.set {fields} (a missing value is null). The app stores all of them or none. When the app refuses the ui.dialog.set, for example because no dialog is open, the call is an error and action is not sent. Then action confirm (OK, ui.dialog.confirm) or cancel (ui.dialog.cancel). Give set, action or both; set holds at most 256 fields. inspect_ui shows the open dialog and its fields as ui.dialog. Returns {dialog, result}: the dialog after the fields were set (or null) and the reply to confirm or cancel (or null). After invoke_menu app.quit, file.close or file.closeAll replies {"pending": "saveChanges"}, {action: "confirm"} saves, {set: [{field: "discard", value: true}], action: "confirm"} closes without saving and {action: "cancel"} cancels the close or quit.
invoke_menu{command, params?}Invokes a menu item by command id. Includes UI commands such as view.* and window.* in remote mode.
open_panel{panel}Remote only. panel is a panel id (layers, swatches, colorGuide, …, as window.panel takes) or its display label ("Color Guide"), in any case.
screenshot{path?, scale?, artboard?, window?, max_side?, traceViews?}Returns MCP image content (image/png, base64) plus a text block. Renders the artboard with the pixels of a PNG export with background: "white" at the same scale; traceViews:true draws Image Trace objects in the view set with imageTrace.setView, as in the app's window; window:true captures the app window (remote only), as ui_screenshot does, and max_side (1–4096, window captures only) scales the returned image down as described for ui_screenshot. With path the backend (the headless session or the app) writes the PNG there too; a window capture written there keeps its full size with max_side.
open_file{path}Opens any readable file as a new active document: .vectorcraft/.drawcraft, .vctemplate, .svg/.svgz, .pdf/.ai, .ait, .eps/.epsf, .ps, .dxf, .emf, .wmf, PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, TIFF, BMP, Targa (.tga/.vda/.icb/.vst), PSD/PSB (an image opens as a document of its pixel size; a Photoshop file as its merged image). Templates (.vctemplate, .ait) open as a new untitled document. PDF, .ai and SVG files saved with Preserve Editing reopen as the document they carry. The reply is document.open's: its warnings say what didn't come in as it was (an EPS whose PostScript can't be read opens as its preview, and the warning names the PostScript error, the operator and the procedure). run_command document.formats lists the formats.
save_file{path?, compress?, version?, preview?, options?}Runs document.save: the document's own file in its own format (native .vectorcraft unless it was opened from or saved as SVG, PDF or a restorable .ai; then warnings say what that format loses). A path's extension picks the format (.vectorcraft, .vctemplate, .pdf, .svg, .svgz, .ai: a PDF carrying the native document, which reopens editable). For a native file, compress: true gzips it (default: the useCompression preference, Preferences › File Handling › Use Compression), version (1 to 4) writes it for an older VectorCraft (1 and 2 are never compressed, and version: 2 with compress: true is an error), and preview: true embeds a PNG of the first artboard. options passes the format's save options as run_command document.formats lists them. When compress, version or preview is given both at the top level and in options with different values, the call is an error (`compress: true` and `options.compress: false` disagree: give the option once) and nothing is written. The save drops each option the written format doesn't read. The reply's warnings then name each such option given (for example `compress` was not used: SVG files don't read it for an .svg path, and `options.preview` was not used: … for a key in options; a .ai file reads compress but not version or preview). run_command document.save and the other save commands refuse any option given beside the path and in options with different values, and give the same warning for a top-level compress, version or preview. The headless session and the app receive the same app.save params, and the app is also sent wait: true: the call returns once the file is written, or with the error (such as a path outside the write root), even with Background Save on. When the save asks first (the question before replacing a file that opened with losses, or on macOS and Linux the file dialog for an untitled document without path), the reply is {pending} or "cancelled" as before, and the save that runs after the answer uses the Background Save preference.
export{path?, format?, scale?, artboard?, range?, selection?, outlineText?, options?}svg, svgz, pdf, ai (the PDF-compatible .ai that Save writes, which carries the document when every artboard is written in order), eps, dxf, emf, wmf, png, jpg, webp, gif, png8 (an indexed .png), tiff, bmp, tga, psd (layered), txt (the document's text), vectorcraft or template (a native template). The tool's format enum and document.formats list them, generated from the engine's format table. When format is omitted, it comes from the path's extension. PDF and .ai write one page per artboard: all of them, or artboard (0-based) / range ("1-3, 5", 1-based). SVG and SVGZ write one file per chosen artboard. EPS writes one file of the visible art; with artboard, range, options.artboards or options: {"useArtboards": true} it writes one file per chosen artboard, after a central file of the visible art when there are two or more, and useArtboards: false keeps the one file. Rasters, DXF, EMF and WMF write one artboard, or one file per chosen artboard with options: {"useArtboards": true}. Rasters also take options: {"rect": [x, y, width, height]} (page points, y down): that area of the page at the given ppi/scale, without an artboard and without changing the document; it is refused with artboard, artboards, range or useArtboards, with a non-positive or non-finite size, and for non-raster formats. options carries more format options (e.g. {"quality": 80} for JPEG). selection: true exports the selected objects cropped to their bounds (the reply adds their bounds, and reports format and the encoder's warnings as a whole-document export does); outlineText: true writes SVG text as paths. Template layers are left out, live effects are kept, and exporting vectorcraft never changes the document's path. Without path the bytes come back as dataBase64. Both backends run the same document.export call.
add_text{text, x?, y?, width?, height?, fit?, fitMinPercent?, path?, mode?, pathEffect?, size?, font?, color?, justify?, vertical?}Point type at (x, y) (justify: left, center or right starts, centres or ends each line on x); area type with width/height (fit: none, autoHeight, shrinkText, as text.areaOptions); or path + mode (area/onPath) to flow text in or along a path, with pathEffect (rainbow, skew, 3dRibbon, stairStep, gravity); justify (also auto, justifyAll) aligns the paragraphs of every kind, as text.setStyle does later. vertical: true uses the existing vertical writing mode for every kind (default false, horizontal). Returns {id}, with overflow: true when the text doesn't fit its frame or path.
apply_effect{effect?, params?, ids?}Appends a live effect. Without effect, returns the effect catalogue with parameters and defaults.
pathfinder{operation, ids?}unite, minusFront, intersect, exclude, divide, trim, merge, crop, outline, minusBack. For a live version, apply the pathfinder.* effect to a group (effect.apply groups several loose selected objects first).
transform{ids?, dx?, dy?, rotate?, scale?, scaleX?, scaleY?, reflect?, shear?, origin?, copy?}Runs move, rotate, scale, reflect, shear in that order. With copy, the first step duplicates.
create_graph{type?, x, y, width, height, csv? | series?, categories?, rows?}The nine Illustrator graph types: column, stacked column, bar, stacked bar, line, area, scatter, pie and radar. An empty CSV cell or a null in rows is a blank value; a number in straight quotes is a label. Edit later with graph.setData / graph.setType via run_command. graph.column {design, columnType: vertical|uniform|repeating|sliding, each, fractions: chop|scale} draws the columns or bars of the series picked with Group Selection (or seriesIndexes, or all of them) with a graph design saved by graph.design; with no design it returns the series' current design and options. Type in the design that holds a % followed by two digits shows each column's value, with at least the first digit's places before the decimal point and the second digit's places after it.
text_wrap{ids?, offset?, invert?, release?}Area type below the objects (same layer) flows around them.
undo / redo{}Many calls that change nothing record no undo step, for example object.align with nothing to move, object.move by 0 without copy and object.showAll with nothing hidden. undo (edit.undo) after such a call undoes the step before it. inspect_document lists the undo steps as history. run_command with edit.clearHistory removes every undo and redo step of the active document and returns {cleared}, the number of steps removed; the document stays as it is, and the command records no undo step.

For example, create editable Japanese area type with the engine's vertical writing mode:

{"name":"add_text","arguments":{"text":"こんにちは","x":40,"y":40,"width":160,"height":240,"size":20,"vertical":true}}

vertical also applies to point type and to path + mode (area or onPath). Omit it or pass false for horizontal writing. This is the same option as text.create / text.createInPath; it does not change the engine's existing glyph orientation or CJK composition.

For SVG/SVGZ, export's top-level outlineText: true writes text as paths; DXF takes it too (its type is outlined anyway unless preserve: editability). PDF instead uses options: {"advanced": {"outlineText": true}}; its default is false (live text).

With mods.shift, a marquee dragged with the Selection tool toggles the objects it reaches (the selected ones leave the selection, the others join it, the rest stays), and one dragged with Direct or Group Selection toggles the anchors inside it (a path left with none leaves the selection, one with all of them is selected whole). The Lasso adds anchors with mods.shift and takes them away with mods.alt. They run select.toggle {ids} and select.anchorsMany {items, mode: "set"|"add"|"toggle"|"subtract"}:

{"name":"pointer_gesture","arguments":{"tool":"selection","mods":{"shift":true},"events":[{"kind":"down","x":180,"y":80},{"kind":"drag","x":300,"y":200},{"kind":"up","x":380,"y":200}]}}

press_key {key: "e"} while a Selection or Group Selection marquee is being dragged makes the marquee select only the objects that lie entirely inside it, measured by their geometric bounds, and e pressed again switches it back to the default mode. The Selection tool selects a group only when the whole group lies inside; Group Selection selects the objects inside groups, each as a whole object. Both run select.set {ids} (select.toggle {ids} with mods.shift). The marquee also selects the artboard guides that lie entirely inside it, and no canvas guide. Each marquee starts in the default mode. To press the key during a drag, end one pointer_gesture after the drag event and send the rest in another with the same tool, which keeps the drag:

{"name":"pointer_gesture","arguments":{"tool":"selection","events":[{"kind":"down","x":80,"y":80},{"kind":"drag","x":220,"y":170}]}}
{"name":"press_key","arguments":{"key":"e"}}
{"name":"pointer_gesture","arguments":{"tool":"selection","events":[{"kind":"drag","x":230,"y":170},{"kind":"up","x":230,"y":170}]}}

With nothing being dragged, e chooses the tool bound to E.

Appearance stacks: an object can carry several fills and strokes (appearance.addFill, appearance.addStroke), indexed in paint order (0 is painted first, the bottom row of the Appearance panel). Cmd+/ runs appearance.addFill and Cmd+Alt+/ runs appearance.addStroke (press_key {key: "/", mods: {cmd: true, alt: true}}). paint.setFill, paint.setStroke, stroke.set, stroke.setAdvanced, paint.editGradient, paint.setGradientGeom and transparency.set take item to edit one of them; run_command appearance.setActiveItem {"index": n} makes that row the target of later calls that omit item (as clicking the row in the Appearance panel does) until the selection changes; the proxy of its kind (paint.proxies) and the Gradient tool's annotator then show that row. inspect_document reports it as paint.appearanceItem. Live effects take the same item to apply to one fill or stroke instead of the whole object: run_command effect.apply {"effect": "path.offsetPath", "item": 0}, and effect.remove, effect.setParams (visible toggles one) and effect.duplicate address that item's effects; apply_effect uses the active item. effect.list reports each object's item effects under applied[].items.

effect.apply {"effect": "path.outlineObject"} (Effect › Path › Outline Object) takes no parameters. Type that carries it is drawn, exported and expanded as its glyph outlines and stays editable type, and the effects after it reshape those outlines. On paths, compound paths and images it does not change what is drawn.

Errors (unknown tool, bad arguments, a disabled or failing command) come back as a normal result with isError: true and a message. The model can read the message and retry.

Swatches go through run_command: swatch.list lists every swatch with its colour group, kind and colour; swatch.edit {name, newName?, color?, mode?, global?, spot?} edits one (fills, strokes and text linked to a global or spot swatch follow its colour and name, as one undo step); swatch.delete {names, unlink?} deletes swatches and colour groups in one undo step (art using a deleted global swatch keeps its colour, unlinked). swatch.new {color?, mode?, spot?, global?, group?} saves a colour (into a colour group with group), gradient or pattern; swatch.newGroup {fromArtwork: true, toGlobal?, includeTints?} makes a colour group of the selected art's colours (by default as global swatches the art links to). swatch.move {names, to?, group?} reorders swatches or moves them into or out of a colour group (as dragging them in the Swatches panel does); give colour group names instead to reorder the groups. Dropping a swatch on art in the app runs paint.setFill (or paint.setStroke, the active proxy) with ids: [the object under the pointer]. The Swatches panel menu's commands: swatch.addUsedColors {selection?, global?}, swatch.unused (a query: the names Select All Unused selects), swatch.merge {names} (the first is kept), swatch.ungroup {name} and swatch.sortByKind. brush.move {name | names, to?, type?} reorders brushes among the brushes of their type, as dragging one onto another of its type in the Brushes panel does. to is an index among the brushes of that type in brush.list order: the brushes go before the first brush of that type at or after to that is not moving, and after the last brush of that type when there is none or to is left out. A move that keeps the order records no undo step. Brushes of different types, or a type other than theirs, are refused with "brushes move only within their type". Document colour mode: file.new {colorMode: "cmyk"} starts a CMYK document with CMYK default swatches, and RGB colors applied to it (paint.setFill/setStroke colors, gradient stops and freeform points, paint.editGradient stops, paint.freeform.addPoint/setPoint, paint.sampleColor, object.mesh.setPointColor/addLine, the text.create and text.createInPath color, the text.setStyle fill, text.setRangeStyle fills and strokes, a shapeBuilder.merge hex fill (its fill: {color, keepModel: true} form keeps RGB), the symbol.adjust stain color, swatch.new {color} and {colors}, swatch.newGroup, swatch.editGroup and swatch.edit) are stored as CMYK, converted through the color settings (edit.colorSettings: the CMYK working space and intent, as Document Color Mode converts them); Gray stays Gray, and keepModel: true keeps a color as given. A color that swatch.edit or swatch.editGroup is given and the swatch already has keeps its model, as does a stop color that paint.editGradient is given and the gradient already has; a stop that names a swatch takes the swatch's color as it is, and swatch.new or swatch.edit with mode converts a color into that mode only. RGB documents keep colors as given. Harmonies, Edit Colors blends, inversions and Recolor Artwork keep each colour's model (a blend between models takes the document's). swatch.new {colors: [...]} saves several colours as swatches in one undo step. color.harmony {color, rule, steps?, variation?, amount?} answers what the Color Guide panel shows: the harmony rule's colours, base first, and per colour its row of 2·steps+1 variations (shades, cool or muted on the left, tints, warm or vivid on the right). With limitTo (a swatch library id or name, or "document" for the document's swatches) every colour snaps to the library's nearest colour (ΔE 2000), as the panel's Limit to Library does (ui.colorGuideLimit); Recolor Artwork's limitTo takes the same values.

Color Themes (local only: no online service) keeps five-colour themes in the preferences. colorTheme.save {colors: [...]} saves up to five colours as they are, colorTheme.save {color, rule} saves the five-colour theme a harmony rule makes from a base colour (base first; name? defaults to "Theme N", replace: "<name>" overwrites that theme in place). colorTheme.list answers {themes: [{name, colors: [hex], keys: [colour keys], rule?}]}, colorTheme.delete {name} removes one and colorTheme.addToSwatches {name} adds one to the Swatches panel as a colour group (one undo step).

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"colorTheme.save","params":{"color":"#2266aa","rule":"splitComplementary","name":"Harbor"}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"colorTheme.addToSwatches","params":{"name":"Harbor"}}}

SVG Options: export to SVG takes them in options, flat or as {"svg": {…}}: styling (presentation, style, entities, css), outlineText, images (embed, or link: embedded images are written next to the SVG, or returned as linked), objectIds (layerNames, minimal, unique), decimals (1–7), minify, responsive, useArtboards, range: "all" (one SVG per artboard, listed in files, each holding only the art over its artboard), preserveEditing (the SVG reopens as the full document), metadata and fewerTspans (one <tspan> per line of type). Unknown keys inside svg are rejected; run_command document.formats lists every option with its default. encoding is utf8, utf16 (big-endian after a byte order mark) or latin1 (ISO 8859-1, other characters as &#x…; references); document.serialize still answers text, plus dataBase64 (the file) when it isn't UTF-8, and such files open again. profile: "tiny12" writes a simplified SVG Tiny 1.2 (presentation attributes only; filters, masks, blend modes and embedded fonts left out, symbols as art, each with a warning). embedFonts: true embeds the fonts type uses as @font-face rules subset to the characters used; a font whose licence (OS/2 fsType) forbids subsetting is embedded whole, one that forbids embedding is left out with a warning. Type names its faces by numeric font-weight (600 for Semibold and for a Japanese face's W6, 300 for Light, bold for 700), embedded or not. svgz takes the same options and writes the SVG gzipped (.svgz files also open, place and paste). run_command document.save {path: "x.svg", svg: {…}} saves as SVG (or .svgz).

Symbols export as one <symbol> with a <use> per instance. An instance the def can't stand for is written as its own art: one stained by its fill; one scaled while the symbol has strokes (their weight doesn't scale), or rotated or scaled while it has effects, brushes or live objects; and every instance of a symbol with pattern paints or unlinked opacity masks (those stay on the page). hiddenLayers: true keeps hidden layers and objects, not displayed (display="none"); document.save and the CLI's convert keep them unless told otherwise, other exports leave them out, and they reopen hidden. An object's own data (object.setProps {data: {pivot: "100,180"}}) is written as data-* attributes (data-pivot="100,180"), and an SVG's data-* attributes come in as it. Attributes in other applications' XML namespaces (plotter:pen="2") come in on their objects too and are written back, each namespace declared once on the root. A preserveEditing SVG carries the native document (CDATA in <metadata>) and a hash of the markup around it: if another app changed the SVG since, document.open reads it as plain SVG and says so in warnings.

Saving beyond save_file goes through run_command: file.saveAs {path?, format?, options?, svg?} (the document takes on the new file and format; for format: "ait", or a .ait path without format, it fails with an error that names file.saveAsTemplate), file.saveCopy (the document keeps its path, title and modified state), file.saveAsTemplate {path?, format?} (it writes a .vctemplate, or, for format: "ait" or a .ait path, a PDF-compatible .ait written as a .ai file is, and with aiVersion: "7" it fails with an error; both open untitled; a path whose extension names another format has it replaced by the template's extension, and any other path that does not end in that extension has it added, so card.png is saved as card.vctemplate and card as card.vctemplate; the suggested name and the Templates folder come back without a path), file.newFromTemplate {path}, file.revert and file.formatOptions {format?} (a format's options with the values a save would use). Without a path the save commands return {dataBase64, name, folder?, warnings}. A document saved as SVG or PDF remembers its options, so the next save reuses them. A PDF opened with pages, cropTo or password is only part of the file, so Save asks for a new name instead of writing it back. file.documentColorMode {mode, convert?, intent?, grays?} switches the document colour mode, converting the colours of the art, symbols, pattern tiles and swatches through the colour settings (object.convertDocumentColorMode is an alias kept for older scripts). Gray colours stay Gray and print on the black plate only. RGB greys are colours like any other: to CMYK they separate through the profile into four-colour greys and a rich black, as Illustrator converts them. grays: "black" puts RGB greys with R = G = B on the black plate instead, K = their grey value (the inks Edit Colors › Convert to Grayscale before the switch would give); other colours convert as usual.

SVG import keeps what the canvas edits live. <pattern> becomes a pattern swatch. A <symbol> with <use> becomes a symbol (named after its data-name, else its id) with an instance per <use>; a <use> that shows it differently (paint inherited from the <use>, a viewport that cuts the art) or that the canvas would draw otherwise (a scaled symbol with strokes) stays art. feGaussianBlur, feDropShadow and the usual shadow chains (offset, blur, flood or colour matrix, composite, merge) become Gaussian Blur, Drop Shadow, Outer Glow, Inner Glow and Feather effects; other filters are listed in warnings. Undisplayed objects (display:none) come back hidden, nested clip paths intersect, and reflected or repeated gradients (spreadMethod) are expanded into stops. <image> files (relative links are found in the SVG's folder; only files on this computer or the SVG's network share are read, and pasted SVG reads only absolute paths) stay linked images (see Linked images); SVG files and data: SVGs become art; a file that can't be read becomes a placeholder in its box, named in warnings and missingLinks.

Native files: run_command document.save {compress: true} writes a gzip-compressed .vectorcraft (the useCompression preference makes that the default for saves; compressed files open like any other, told apart by their content). version: 2 or 1 writes a file older VectorCraft versions open (format name drawcraft, never compressed), and preview: true embeds a PNG of the first artboard, at most 256 px on its longer side. A save writes a temporary file and renames it over the old one, so a failed save never damages the file; only the images the document uses are written, and document keys written by a newer version are kept. An uncompressed file is indented JSON when the document has at most 2,000 layers and objects and the indented document, as the chosen version writes it, is at most 1 MiB; otherwise the file is compact JSON.

Resources

Fixed resources and the four templates (same as Resource templates under Protocol):

URIContent
vectorcraft://documentdocument.inspect summary (JSON)
vectorcraft://document/jsonThe complete document model (JSON)
vectorcraft://commandsLive command catalog with enabled state
vectorcraft://object/{id}One layer or object (document.node {id, summary: true})
vectorcraft://command/{id}One command's metadata and enablement
vectorcraft://effect/{id}One live effect with parameters and defaults
vectorcraft://swatch/{name}One swatch (percent-encode spaces)

Examples

Draw, look, export:

{"name":"draw_shape","arguments":{"shape":"rectangle","x":72,"y":72,"width":200,"height":120,"radius":12,"fill":"#1e88e5","stroke":"none"}}
{"name":"draw_shape","arguments":{"shape":"star","cx":400,"cy":300,"radius1":90,"radius2":40,"points":5,"fill":"#ffc107","stroke":"#000000","strokeWidth":2}}
{"name":"draw_path","arguments":{"d":"M72 400 C 150 300 250 500 330 400","stroke":"#e53935","strokeWidth":4,"fill":"none"}}
{"name":"screenshot","arguments":{"scale":0.5}}
{"name":"export","arguments":{"path":"/tmp/art.svg"}}
{"name":"export","arguments":{"path":"/tmp/art.png","options":{"ppi":144,"background":"white","antiAlias":"type"}}}
{"name":"export","arguments":{"path":"/tmp/icon.png","range":"1-3","options":{"useArtboards":true}}}

Export As goes through options too: for PNG, JPEG and WebP useArtboards: true writes one file per artboard (all, or range), named <file>-<artboard>.<ext>, and returns {path, files} (a PDF keeps them as pages of one file; an SVG writes one file per artboard of a range); useArtboards: false exports the bounds of the visible art with what its effects paint, the effects of groups, type and images included. artboard.fitToArt measures the art the same way.

Raster exports take more options in options: ppi (72, 150, 300…; stored in the file), background (transparent, white, black or "#rrggbb"), antiAlias (none, art, type: text snapped to pixels) and, for PNG, interlaced.

Long-tail commands:

{"name":"list_commands","arguments":{"filter":"align"}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"select.all"}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"object.align","params":{"horizontal":"left"}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"object.group"}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"document.exportPdf","params":{"path":"/tmp/art.pdf","range":"1, 3","compatibility":"1.5"}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"document.pdfSettings","params":{"marks":{"trim":true},"includeDocument":true}}}

PDF files take the Save PDF dialog's options: preset, standard, compatibility, the General toggles and the compression, marks, bleed, output, advanced and security sections (list_commands with filter exportPdf documents every field). document.exportPdf and export (format pdf, the options in options) return warnings: options accepted but not applied yet, and features approximated or left out. An image whose pixel data can't be decoded (a PNG cut short) is left out of the pages, and warnings holds the note "an image couldn't be decoded and is missing from the picture" once, as raster exports give it; .ai saves and file.print do the same. A standard with a PDF version it doesn't allow is refused (PDF/A-2b at 1.3 or 2.0, PDF/X-4 above 1.6, PDF/X-1a and PDF/X-3 above 1.4); choosing a standard without giving compatibility sets its version (PDF 1.3 for PDF/X-1a and PDF/X-3). A security password encrypts the file (RC4 40-bit at PDF 1.3, RC4 128-bit at 1.4–1.5, AES-128 at 1.6, AES-256 at 1.7 and 2.0) with its permissions; either password opens it (document.open {password}), and a password with PDF/A or PDF/X is refused. Pattern fills and strokes are written as their tiles clipped to the area they paint (a stroke's outline, with its dashes, caps, profile, arrowheads and alignment), and freeform gradients as an image of their colour field at the document's raster effects resolution, clipped the same way. createLayers writes each layer and sublayer (template layers are left out) as a PDF layer, an optional content group named as the layer and listed under its parent layer's: hidden layers are off (their art is written, for the reader to show), non-printing ones have /PrintState /OFF and locked ones are locked. It needs PDF 1.5 or later (at 1.4 it warns), and the file reopens with those layers and sublayers. A .ai file writes them unless createLayers is false, so apps that read its PDF part find the layers, hidden ones included; hidden objects aren't in the PDF part. Images follow the compression settings of their kind (color, gray, or mono for black-and-white images): above abovePpi as placed they are resampled (downsample: average, subsample or bicubic) to ppi, and compressed with zip, jpeg (at quality; images with transparency stay lossless) or auto (JPEGs stay JPEG, the others lossless). none, jpeg2000, CCITT and runLength are written as ZIP, with a warning when an image needs them. A 16-bit PNG that is not resampled, recompressed or converted keeps its 16 bits a sample in PDF 1.5 and later files, and is written at 8 bits in PDF 1.3 and 1.4 files. CMYK images stay CMYK (DeviceCMYK, or ICC-based with the CMYK profile when colours are tagged, as in PDF/X-3 and PDF/X-4): a CMYK JPEG neither resampled nor recompressed is written unchanged, and the others' ink amounts are resampled and compressed again (CMYK JPEG or ZIP). output.conversion converts them like CMYK colours: destination to another CMYK profile or to RGB, preserveNumbers (and PDF/X-1a) keeps their numbers in a CMYK destination. document.pdfSettings lists the options that differ from the preset and the warnings without writing a file. thumbnails: true embeds each page drawn small (106 px on its long side, without the layers the page leaves out) as its /Thumb image. fastWebView: true writes a linearised file (the linearization dictionary first, then the first page with hint tables saying where every other page's objects are), which stays linearised when a password encrypts it. compatibility: "1.3" writes a PDF 1.3 file, which has no transparency: a copy of the document is flattened first with flattenerPreset (empty: High Resolution; high, medium, low or a saved preset, flattener.presets.list) and flattener options over it (as object.flattenTransparency takes them); images with see-through pixels count as transparency and rasterized areas are clipped to their regions. A file that would still have transparency fails the export. PDF/X-1a and PDF/X-3 files are PDF 1.3 files too. A page with a side longer than 14,400 pt is written with a UserUnit (PDF 1.6): its longer side divided by 14,400, rounded up to the next thousandth, with the page's sizes and coordinates in those units, so that no side is longer than 14,400 units (ISO 32000-1, Annex C.2). In PDF 1.3 to 1.5 files and PDF/X files, such a page is written in points.

An Illustrator EPS (version 9 on) or .ai file opens from the editing data it carries (after the EPS page; in the .ai file's PDF private data): layers and sublayers (name, order, visibility, lock, printing, preview, dimming, colour), groups as they were nested, compound paths, clipping groups, object names, hidden and locked objects, fills and strokes (spot colours as spot swatches), linear and radial gradients, opacity, blend modes, isolation and knockout, embedded images with their alpha channel, guides, and every artboard where it is. An object with several fills or strokes, effects or a brush comes in as its drawn look (a group named after it). Type is made from the file's text document, whole and editable: point type, area type (a story in several frames as threaded type) and type on a path, with its fonts, size, leading, tracking, scaling, baseline shift, fill and stroke (grey, RGB or CMYK), opacity, blend mode, alignment, indents and paragraph spacing. Where the text document can't say, type that shows is the page's, in its text object's place. A text object on a layer that shows and doesn't print keeps the type the text document makes for it, on that layer, even where the page doesn't draw it. A file whose editing data can't be decompressed or has symbols, pattern fills, placed files or anything else the reader doesn't read on a layer that shows, or whose layers look different from its page, opens as before: an EPS as its printed page, a .ai file from its PDF content (where plain groups open ungrouped), with a warning saying why. Layers look different when they draw an object the page doesn't (or miss one it draws), and, for an EPS whose page is the box of its art, when they print art outside it. A file whose editing data is for a page of another size opens the same way, and its warning gives both sizes in points, with the scale of the editing data's page to the file's page (1:10 for a tenth of its size) when their sides are in proportion. With textAs: "outlines" a .ai file whose type shows opens from its PDF content, which has the type's outlines. A PDF saved with Illustrator's editing data holds it in AIPDFPrivateData streams and opens from it as a .ai file does.

The document then has what the file's page doesn't print: hidden objects and layers, layers that don't print, guides and the art outside the artboards, as the app that wrote the file shows them. Exports and artboard.fitToArt leave out the hidden art, the guides and template layers; an EPS's artboard is the file's artboard, not the page's bounding box. A print pipeline that wants only what the page prints opens the file with editingData: false (default true): an EPS as its page, a .ai file from its PDF content (a .ai saved without PDF compatibility then can't be opened).

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"document.open","params":{"path":"/tmp/label.eps","editingData":false}}}

Opening a PDF (or .ai) imports every page as an artboard and layer; document.open takes pages ("2-3, 5", 1-based), cropTo (bounding (the art's bounds), art, crop (default), trim, bleed, media: the box each artboard gets; with trim the bleed box round it becomes the document's bleed, and a PDF-compatible .ai or a PDF saved with its editing data opens on its trim boxes for crop, its crop box taking the bleed in) and password for an encrypted file. document.pdfInfo reads a file without opening it: the page count, each page's size and boxes, needsPassword, and with thumbnail: n a PNG of page n. It reads a PostScript file (an EPS, a .ps file, a .ai in PostScript form) as one page the size of its bounding box (a US Letter page without one), with an empty boxes, and thumbnail: 1 shows what that page prints. A page whose UserUnit (PDF 1.6) is above 1 opens at its size in points, the file's numbers times the UserUnit (one above 75,000 counts as 75,000), and document.pdfInfo gives its size and boxes in points too. A .ai whose editing data has its artboards in the units of such a page opens from that data at the page's size. Imported colours keep their model: DeviceCMYK and CMYK ICC colours stay CMYK (a file painted mostly in CMYK opens as a CMYK document), DeviceGray is Gray, and Separation and DeviceN inks become spot swatches the art links to at its tint (gradient stops too). CMYK images (DeviceCMYK, or ICC-based with four components) keep their ink amounts: a CMYK JPEG as it is, any other as a CMYK TIFF (masked CMYK images, and ones with 1, 2 or 4 bits per sample, open in RGB with a warning). Placing a CMYK TIFF keeps it CMYK too. colorMode: "rgb" | "cmyk" opens any file in that mode instead, its colours converted as file.documentColorMode does (grays too):

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"document.pdfInfo","params":{"path":"/tmp/brochure.pdf","thumbnail":2,"cropTo":"trim"}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"document.open","params":{"path":"/tmp/brochure.pdf","pages":"2-3","cropTo":"trim"}}}

PostScript files (.eps, .epsf, .ps, and .ai files saved in older formats or without PDF compatibility) open through the EPS reader (see EPS and PostScript import). An .ai saved without PDF compatibility (its PDF part is only a placeholder page) opens from its editing data alone: its type is made from the file's text document where it can be (point type, area type and type on a path), and what can't be is left out with a warning. Non-native art (the content of a placed PDF, which Illustrator shows but doesn't edit and keeps as a PDF inside the editing data) is drawn by the PDF importer, as a group named "Non-native art" fitted to the object's box; one whose PDF can't be read is left out with a warning. Without editing data it says it can't be opened. pages that name every page of a file saved without PDF compatibility also open it from its editing data. Without editing data it says it can't be opened. pages that name every page of such a file also open it from its editing data. file.place reads it from its editing data too and places the art of the layers that show on artboard page, clipped to that artboard unless crop is bounding. clipboard.importPdf takes every object of its editing

What a PDF holds comes in as editable art: soft masks become opacity masks (an alpha mask as a white copy of its art; the backdrop colour gives Clip, an inverting transfer function Invert), transparency groups keep isolation and knockout, tiling patterns become pattern swatches, patch and triangle mesh shadings become gradient meshes, and gradients keep their stop opacity and stop where the shading doesn't extend. Text becomes point type, one object per run of a line in the file's font (by name; fonts that aren't available are listed in warnings and show in the fallback font, and every export that draws that type — PDF, EPS, EMF/WMF, raster images, SVG with outlined or embedded fonts — says in its warnings that it wrote the fallback font) — textAs: "outlines" keeps the glyph outlines the file draws instead (its embedded fonts, installed or not). Lines wrapped in a frame (each wrapped line ending in a space, sharing a left edge, font and leading) come back as one area type object, left aligned or justified, where laying it out breaks the lines where the file does; a stroke written as each glyph's outline stroked is the type object's stroke; glyphs set apart by gaps wider than a space keep their places. Strokes stay live strokes (width, cap, join, miter limit, dash and paint), and an object written as a fill and then a stroke of the same outline is one path with both. Optional content groups (the layers of PDF and PDF-compatible .ai files) become layers with their name, visibility (the default configuration's, or a view state that is off), print state and lock, nested as sublayers the way the file's layer order nests them, stacked as the pages mark them (layers with no art come in empty); art that is off comes in as a hidden layer. Art outside them goes to a layer per page (except the opaque white page a .ai paints under its layers, which isn't art). layers: false gives one layer per page of only what shows:

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"document.open","params":{"path":"/tmp/map.pdf","textAs":"outlines","layers":false}}}

Preserve Editing (preserveEditing, on in the VectorCraft Default preset) embeds the native document in the PDF as an embedded file (vectorcraft-editing.vectorcraft; files named drawcraft-editing.drawcraft are read too) with a hash of the pages it was written with. open_file / document.open of such a PDF, .ai or .ait (no pages picked) restores the document exactly (restored: true); when another app changed the pages, or the data can't be read, the artwork is imported instead and the first warning says why. Choosing a standard turns it off (PDF/A refuses it). Save to a .ai path (document.save {path: "art.ai"} or {format: "ai"}) writes a PDF-compatible file that always carries the document, takes the PDF options and keeps its path when reopened, so Save writes .ai again; a .ait opens untitled. document.export / serialize to .ai (and the CLI's run --export x.ai and convert in.svg out.ai) write the same file without giving the document a path. options: {aiVersion: "7"} writes legacy .ai files of version 7 instead (the PostScript form, which keeps layers, groups, compound paths and clipping groups in apps that read it), one per artboard of range (default all; {name}-{artboard}.ai when several, listed in the result's files), with transparentImages: "flatten" (default) or "omit" for images with see-through pixels, opacity or a blending mode, hiddenObjects: "layer" (default: a hidden layer of each layer's hidden objects), "omit" or "visible", and outlineText: true to write type as outlines instead of text objects. An image that can't be decoded is left out of the file and does not show in art that is flattened or whose raster effects are expanded; warnings carries "an image couldn't be decoded and is missing from the picture" once for all such images. From the CLI: vectorcraft-cli convert in.svg out.ai --ai-version 7 [--hidden-objects layer|omit|visible] [--transparent-images flatten|omit] [--outline-text] [--range R]. document.export, serialize, exportSelection and exportForScreens refuse a .ait path or format: "ait" with an error that names file.saveAsTemplate, which writes it. The CLI's run --export x.ait and convert in.svg out.ait write it as file.saveAsTemplate does, with every artboard; convert refuses --artboard and --range for a .ait.

Drive a tool like a mouse:

{"name":"pointer_gesture","arguments":{"tool":"ellipse","events":[
  {"kind":"down","x":100,"y":100},{"kind":"drag","x":150,"y":140},{"kind":"up","x":200,"y":180}]}}

Edit a gradient with the Gradient tool's annotator (the bar runs along the vector; stop chips sit 10 px under it, midpoint diamonds 7 px above): a click on the bar adds a stop, dragging a chip moves it (drag it off the bar to delete it, hold Alt to copy it), dragging the end handle changes the vector. The selected stop (gradient.selectStop) takes Delete and ←/→:

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"paint.setFill","params":{"gradient":{"start":[100,150],"end":[200,150]}}}}
{"name":"pointer_gesture","arguments":{"tool":"gradient","events":[{"kind":"down","x":130,"y":150},{"kind":"up","x":130,"y":150}]}}
{"name":"pointer_gesture","arguments":{"events":[{"kind":"down","x":130,"y":160},{"kind":"drag","x":160,"y":160},{"kind":"up","x":160,"y":160}]}}
{"name":"press_key","arguments":{"key":"Delete"}}

A radial gradient's annotator also draws its extent: a dashed ellipse around the centre (the start, drawn as a ring) with a dot on it across the bar (drag it to change the aspect ratio); dragging the ellipse elsewhere rotates it. The dot inside the centre ring is the focal point, where the first stop sits: drag it for an off-centre radial, back onto the centre to centre it. paint.setGradientGeom sets the same things directly (aspect in %, focal in document coordinates or null; start/end may be left out), and they export as SVG fx/fy and PDF two-point radial shadings:

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"paint.setGradientGeom","params":{"aspect":60,"focal":[130,140]}}}

Dither (the Gradient panel's box) draws a linear or radial gradient fill with fine noise that hides banding; SVG and PDF exports write such a fill as an image at the Document Raster Effects resolution. It applies to fills (stroke: true is refused):

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"paint.editGradient","params":{"dither":true}}}

Method (the Gradient panel's menu): classic (the default) mixes the colours between stops in display RGB, perceptual mixes a linear or radial gradient's colours in OKLab so the steps look even. SVG and PDF exports write a Perceptual gradient as an image at the Document Raster Effects resolution (a dithered Perceptual fill with its noise). paint.setFill/paint.setStroke take it in gradient too:

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"paint.editGradient","params":{"method":"perceptual"}}}

Freeform gradients: paint.editGradient {kind: "freeform"} places four or more colour points inside each selected object (coloured along the stops; mode: "points"|"lines" is the Draw toggle). paint.freeform.get lists the points (document coordinates), lines and the selected point; paint.freeform.addPoint {at, color?, opacity?, spread?, line?}, setPoint {index?, …}, deletePoint {index?}, addLine {points}, splitLine {line, segment, t?} and selectPoint {index|null} edit them (index defaults to the selected point; each edit is one undo step). The color of a point colored along the stops, of a point splitLine adds and of a point addPoint adds without color is a mix of the colors around it (the two stops, the line's two points, or all the points) in the color model those colors share, so CMYK stops and points give CMYK points. With the Gradient tool on a freeform gradient a click on a point selects it (drag to move it), a click on a line adds a point on it, a click elsewhere on the art adds a point (in Lines mode joined to the selected one), and Delete removes the selected point:

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"paint.editGradient","params":{"kind":"freeform","mode":"lines"}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"paint.freeform.addPoint","params":{"at":[150,150],"color":"#ff3366","spread":20}}}
{"name":"pointer_gesture","arguments":{"tool":"gradient","events":[{"kind":"down","x":180,"y":170},{"kind":"up","x":180,"y":170}]}}

On the canvas the selected point also shows its spread as a dashed ring with a handle 16 px (or the spread, if larger) to its right: drag the ring or handle to change the spread. Dragging a point out of the object removes it, and double-clicking one opens its popover (dialog gradientStop, which on a freeform gradient edits the selected point: set color, opacity and spread and confirm). In Lines mode successive clicks on the art draw one smooth line through the points they add; a click on an existing point first continues the line from it, and press_key Escape (or a click off the art) ends it:

{"name":"pointer_gesture","arguments":{"tool":"gradient","events":[{"kind":"down","x":110,"y":190},{"kind":"up","x":110,"y":190},{"kind":"down","x":150,"y":170},{"kind":"up","x":150,"y":170}]}}
{"name":"press_key","arguments":{"key":"Escape"}}

Raw protocol (for debugging):

printf '%s\n' \
  '{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"method":"initialize","params":{"protocolVersion":"2025-06-18","capabilities":{},"clientInfo":{"name":"sh","version":"0"}}}' \
  '{"jsonrpc":"2.0","method":"notifications/initialized"}' \
  '{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":2,"method":"tools/call","params":{"name":"draw_shape","arguments":{"shape":"ellipse","x":10,"y":10,"width":100,"height":80,"fill":"#ff0000"}}}' \
  '{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":3,"method":"tools/call","params":{"name":"inspect_document","arguments":{}}}' \
  | target/release/vectorcraft-cli mcp --headless

Headless batch CLI
