vectorcraft-cli commands                          # command catalogue (JSON)
vectorcraft-cli run --in in.svg \
  --cmd select.all --cmd paint.setFill --params '{"color":"#ff0000"}' \
  --export out.svg --export out.png --scale 2
vectorcraft-cli run --cmd file.new --params '{"width":800,"height":600}' \
  --cmd shape.star --params '{"cx":400,"cy":300,"radius1":200,"radius2":90}' \
  --export star.vectorcraft

run prints one JSON line per step (open, cmd, export) and exits non-zero on the first failure, naming the step (counting from 0, as "$N" references do) and its command. --params applies to the --cmd just before it. run also accepts the host commands file.open, file.save, file.export, file.exportForScreens and tool.select. run --in, convert and info read every format document.open reads (vectorcraft-cli --help lists them).

Steps from a file or standard input

A command line has a length limit (32,767 characters on Windows), which long parameters such as a placed file's dataBase64 or a long list of ids soon reach (#3147). --commands FILE reads the steps from a file instead, and --commands - from standard input:

vectorcraft-cli run --in sheet.vectorcraft --commands steps.json --export out.pdf

[
  {"command": "file.place", "params": {"name": "code.svg", "dataBase64": "PHN2ZyB4bWxucz0i…", "rect": [20, 40, 16, 57]}},
  {"command": "object.setProps", "params": {"id": "$0.ids.0", "name": "barcode"}}
]

The text is a JSON array of steps, or one step per line (JSON Lines; blank lines are skipped). A step is an object with command, the command id (or id, as command_batch names it), and optional params, a JSON object; any other key is refused. The steps run where --commands stands among the --cmd and --export options, in order, and count with them for "$N", so a file can refer to the ids an earlier --cmd made and a later --cmd to the file's results. --commands may be given more than once (standard input once). The text is untrusted input: it must be UTF-8 (a byte order mark is skipped), at most 64 MiB and 100,000 steps, and a malformed step stops the run before anything runs, with the step's number and its entry (entry 3) or line (line 3) in the message.

What a headless session remembers

vectorcraft-cli run and the headless mcp server keep two things between runs, in folders next to the preferences (the folder the desktop app uses, so both see the same ones; VECTORCRAFT_CONFIG_DIR moves it):

  • Saved PDF presets (pdf.preset.save, .delete, .import): one .vcpdfpresets file per preset in PDF Presets, written as the command runs. Presets saved earlier are there at the next start and work as preset in the PDF options.
  • ICC profiles (color.loadProfile): a copy of the file goes into Color Profiles (the result says remembered: true), and every profile there is registered again at the next start, before the first command, so edit.colorSettings {"cmyk": "…"} or a preset's output intent can name it in a later run. The copy is what counts: the original may move or be deleted. color.removeProfile {name} forgets a profile and its copy (not while it is a working space).

Not remembered: Color Settings, the document, anything else. Nothing is read or written when VECTORCRAFT_NO_PREFS is set, and a server confined with --automation-read-root or --automation-write-root keeps nothing either (the folders are outside its roots). A file that is damaged, too large (a profile over 32 MB, a presets file over 1 MB) or not a valid preset or profile is skipped and logged. When the desktop app starts, the presets it kept in its preferences file before this folder existed are moved into it; after that the folder is the one place they live, so a preset deleted by vectorcraft-cli is gone in the app too.

Reproducible output

PNG, PDF, EPS, legacy .ai, packaged zips and saved .vectorcraft files record the time they were made in (PDF CreationDate and ModDate, the PNG Creation Time entry, a zip entry's date, a document's created and modified), so the same commands run twice gave different bytes though the pixels matched (#2880). Setting SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH to a non-negative whole number of seconds since the epoch records that instant instead of the clock, and the bytes repeat (https://reproducible-builds.org/specs/source-date-epoch/):

SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH=1791208980 vectorcraft-cli run --cmd file.new --params '{}' --export out.pdf

A command that takes its own date (file.new {created}, a save's modified) still wins over the variable. A blank, negative or non-numeric value is ignored and the clock decides, so an export is never refused over the environment. SVG output carries no time and was already reproducible.

Two things stay on the real clock and are not pinned by the variable: the recovery heartbeat, and the random seed PDF encryption draws on. A PDF exported with a password therefore still differs between runs whatever SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH says, because that seed supplies its AES initialisation vectors and its file /ID. Reproducible output means an unencrypted export.

Parameter keys

command_run, each step of command_batch, run --cmd … --params and the control channel's engine.execute check the keys of params against the command's params description (the params text command_list returns): the object it starts with, {x, width?: pt, name?|title?: …}, names the keys; ? marks an optional one. A key it doesn't name is refused before the command runs:

{"id": "file.new", "params": {"widht": 300}}
→ invalid parameters for `file.new`: unknown parameter `widht`; did you mean `width`?

Only the top level is checked: the keys of an object inside a value (artboardLayout: {…}) are the command's to read. The one exception is an effect's params: effect.apply (and appearance.addEffect) and effect.setParams check them against the effect's own entry in effect.list, its defaults and the keys its params text names. A key the effect doesn't take, or a value of another type than its default (a string for a number, a number for a switch), is refused the same way:

{"id": "effect.apply", "params": {"effect": "blur.gaussian", "params": {"blurr": 3}}}
→ invalid parameters for `effect.apply`: unknown parameter `blurr`; the command takes: radius

The color of Drop Shadow, Inner Glow, Outer Glow and Shift to Color must be one the effect can read: a hex color (#rrggbb, #rgb or #rrggbbaa, with or without the #), black, white, red, green or blue, [r, g, b] (three numbers, each read on a 0 to 1 scale), or a color object ({"model": "rgb", "r": 1, "g": 0, "b": 0}). effect.apply and effect.setParams refuse any other value themselves (where the parameter check runs first, a number or a boolean is refused as not a string), so the app's effect dialogs and Appearance panel, a step of command.batch and a replayed journal get the same error, and the document and its undo history stay as they were:

{"id": "effect.setParams", "params": {"index": 0, "params": {"color": "#ff00"}}}
→ invalid parameters for `effect.setParams`: `color` must be a #rrggbb color

A document saved with such a color opens as before, and the effect draws its default color (an array of three or more entries reads an entry that isn't a number as 0).

The channel of Levels and Curves must be rgb, red, green or blue (r, g, b and any letter case are read too). effect.apply and effect.setParams refuse any other value themselves in the same way, and a document saved with one opens as before and draws it as rgb:

{"id": "effect.setParams", "params": {"index": 0, "params": {"channel": "alpha"}}}
→ invalid parameters for `effect.setParams`: `channel` must be one of rgb, red, green, blue

A number outside the range the effect documents is still stored as given and clamped when the effect draws. A plug-in effect's parameters are resolved against its manifest instead, and effect.setParams as a step of command.batch isn't checked before the batch runs (the effect it edits may only exist once the steps before it ran); the command still refuses an effect color it can't read. A description with a … entry takes more keys than it lists: those of the command it names (…settings as file.new), or any key when it names none, as document.export and document.save do for the options of the format they write (one object can carry several formats' options). A command that takes its own reply back (document.setup, text.areaOptions) says so the same way. The app's own journal isn't checked: an action or journal recorded by an older version replays as it did, except for an effect color that the effect can't read.

An unknown command id is matched the same way as an unknown parameter key: the error names the nearest command id when one is near, ignoring case. A step of command.batch or action.batch gets the same suggestion in its step error:

{"id": "select.al"}
→ unknown command `select.al`; did you mean `select.all`?

These Object menu commands refuse a string or another value that is not a number with `scaleH` must be a number, not "50", naming the key, and change nothing: scaleH, scaleV, moveH, moveV and rotate in object.transformEach; ppi in object.rasterize; width, height, spacingX and spacingY in object.createObjectMosaic; offset, length, bleed and weight in object.createTrimMarks; and spacing in artboard.rearrange. A null is read as a key left out. object.cropImage refuses a rect that is not an array of four numbers, such as a string, with rect must be [x, y, width, height]; a null rect crops to the artboard, as a missing one does.

object.createObjectMosaic (columns, rows), object.transformEach (reference, seed) and artboard.rearrange (columns) take whole numbers, written 4 or 4.0. A fraction or a negative number is refused with `columns` must be a whole number. A string or another value that is not a number is refused with `columns` must be a number, not "4". Each message names the key that was given, and the command then changes nothing.

These Object menu commands refuse a string, a number or another value that is not true or false with `copy` must be true or false, naming the key, and change nothing: reflectX, reflectY, random, copy, strokes, corners, objects and patterns in object.transformEach; gray and deleteRaster in object.createObjectMosaic; trim in object.cropImage; allArtboards in object.createTrimMarks; and byColumn, moveArtwork and patterns in artboard.rearrange. A null is read as a key left out. object.createTrimMarks refuses a style that is not a string, such as 5, with `style` must be roman or japanese, the message it gives for a string that names no style.

A whole number that is larger than the build's usize can hold (4294967295 on a 32-bit build, such as the wasm32-unknown-unknown web build) counts as larger than any index, place, byte offset or count. An index, such as a subpath, anchor, segment, document index, artboard, appearance item or index, gradient stop or freeform point index, names nothing, and the command does what it does for any other index past the end: most commands fail and change nothing, and appearance.removeItem removes nothing. A place (to in swatch.move, artboard.reorder, graphicStyle.move, appearance.moveItem, appearance.duplicateItem and effect.move) puts the item last. A text start or end is past the end of the text. object.slice.divide refuses such a rows or columns, recolor.reduce keeps each color in its own row for such a colors, and layer.setProps refuses such a color. object.setBounds reads a reference past 8 as 8, as object.transformEach does. The blend, envelope and gradient mesh commands refuse a string or another value that is not a number with the same message, as in `steps` must be a number, not "8", and change nothing: steps, distance and value in object.blend.make and object.blend.options; bend, h, v, horizontalDistortion and verticalDistortion in object.envelope.makeWithWarp, resetWithWarp and options; rows, cols and columns in object.envelope.makeWithMesh, resetWithMesh and object.mesh.create; fidelity in object.envelope.options; highlight in object.mesh.create; and opacity in object.mesh.setPointColor. A null is read as a key left out. object.mesh.create refuses an at that is not two numbers with `at` must be [x, y].

The transform, Align, Object Properties and Live Shape Properties commands refuse a string, a number or another value that is not true or false with `strokes` must be true or false, naming the key, and leave the document and its undo history as they were: the options copy, strokes, corners, patterns, objects and typeAreas in object.transform, object.move, object.nudge, object.rotate, object.scale, object.reflect, object.shear, object.transformAgain and object.setBounds; anchors in object.transform; big in object.nudge; absolute in object.rotate; proportional in object.setBounds; patterns in object.align, object.distribute and object.distributeSpacing; visible, locked, isolate and knockoutShape in object.setProps; and invertPie, makeSidesEqual and relativeRadii in object.setLiveShape. A null is read as a key left out.

They also refuse a string or another value that is not a number with `opacity` must be a number, not "50", naming the key, and leave the document and its undo history as they were: opacity in object.setProps; radius and sides in object.setLiveShape; reference in object.setBounds; and artboard in object.align, object.distribute and object.distributeSpacing. A null is read as a key left out.

The blend and envelope commands refuse a string, a number or another value that is not true or false with `smooth` must be true or false, naming the key, and leave the document, its undo history and the options new blends and envelopes start with as they were: smooth in object.blend.make and object.blend.options; independent in object.blend.spine.moveAnchor; perpendicular in object.blend.spine.reshapeSegment; horizontal in object.envelope.makeWithWarp, resetWithWarp and options, also when an orientation is given; maintainShape in object.envelope.resetWithMesh; antiAlias, distortAppearance, distortLinearGradients and distortPatternFills in object.envelope.options; and editing in object.envelope.editContents. A null is read as a key left out.

These commands refuse a string, a number or another value that is not true or false with `closed` must be true or false, naming the key, and leave the document and its undo history as they were: clockwise in shape.spiral; closed in shape.arc and path.create; vertical and placeholder in text.create and text.createInPath; underline, strikethrough, allCaps, proportionalMetrics, tateChuYoko and noBreak in text.setRangeStyle; effective in text.getRange; on in type.opticalMarginAlignment, type.systemLayout and type.fractionalWidths; remove and overhang in type.ruby; expandInstances in symbol.delete; alt in symbol.spray and symbol.adjust; spot and global in swatch.new and swatch.edit; unlink in swatch.delete; fromArtwork, toGlobal and includeTints in swatch.newGroup; global and selection in swatch.addUsedColors; visible in appearance.setItem; copy in appearance.transfer; reverse and append in appearance.copyFrom; reverse and stroke in paint.editGradient; stroke in paint.setGradientGeom and in paint.freeform.addPoint, setPoint, deletePoint, addLine, splitLine and get; erase, openFilledAsClosed and splitStroke in shapeBuilder.merge; openFilledAsClosed in shapeBuilder.regions; closeGaps in livePaint.gapOptions; and enhanced in imageTrace.make and imageTrace.makeAndExpand. A null is read as a key left out.

These commands refuse a string or another value that is not a number with `radius` must be a number, not "2", naming the key, and change nothing: radius in shape.rectangle; sides and rotation in shape.polygon; radius2, points and rotation in shape.star; diameter, opacity, brightness, growth, fuzziness, rays, longest, rayFuzziness, rings, largest, x2, y2, direction, pathLength and seed in shape.flare; decay and segments in shape.spiral; rows and columns in shape.rectangularGrid; concentric and radial in shape.polarGrid; size and the width, height and fitMinPercent of area in text.create; size and fitMinPercent in text.createInPath; start, end, size, tracking, baselineShift, hScale, vScale, rotation and strokeWidth in text.setRangeStyle; start and end in text.editRange and text.getRange; at in text.startList; start, hScale, vScale and rotation in text.touchType; by, start and end in type.step; by in type.size.increase and type.size.decrease; size in type.ruby; width, height and fitMinPercent in text.areaOptions; x and y in symbol.place; radius, density and scale in symbol.spray; radius and intensity in symbol.adjust; to in swatch.move; weight and opacity in appearance.setItem; angle in paint.editGradient and in the gradient of a command that takes a paint; t in paint.freeform.splitLine; tolerance and gap in shapeBuilder.merge; gap in shapeBuilder.regions; and width and tolerance in livePaint.strokeEdge. A null is read as a key left out. A gradient stop that names a swatch refuses a tint that is not a number with stop 0: `tint` must be a number.

A step can use an earlier step's result, in run as in the command_batch tool. A parameter string that is exactly $N or $N.path takes that value from the result of step N (counting from 0, in the order they ran; run --in's open isn't a step), and $last… from the step before. A path goes into objects by key and arrays by index: "$1.id", "$2.ids.0", "$last.ids[0]". A string starting with $$ stands for one $ ("$$1.99" is $1.99). A reference that names no value fails the step, so a command never runs with one left in it:

vectorcraft-cli run --cmd file.new --params '{"width":600,"height":200}'   --cmd text.create --params '{"x":10,"y":60,"text":"plain and bold words","size":30}'   --cmd text.setRangeStyle --params '{"id":"$1.id","start":10,"end":14,"style":"Bold"}'

Checking scripts written for the reference app

script.check {path? | data? | dataBase64?, name?, includes?} reads a script written for the reference app (.jsx, .jsxinc, .js, or a compiled .jsxbin) and reports what it uses. The check does not run any of the script, and it does not change the document or the preferences. The reply's kind is script, compiled (a .jsxbin file, or text that starts with @JSXBIN@, which the check doesn't read), module (a file with a top-level import or export, such as a React .jsx file), notText (a NUL byte, or more than one byte in ten a control character, in the first 8 KiB) or tooLarge (over 8 MiB). For a script, the reply holds these lists:

  • needs lists what the script needs from the app that runs it: language (the script language's own objects and directives, such as $, alert, UnitValue and #target), dom (the reference app's document model), files, menuCommands, dialogs, and hostOnly (features only the reference app has: palettes, #targetengine, BridgeTalk, sockets, native code, XMP, the XML object and E4X, operator overloading, reflection, actions and selectTool).
  • directives lists the #include, #includepath, #script, #strict, #target and #targetengine lines, and targets splits each #target line's application specifier into app, instance, version and locale. A directive is read where it starts a line, written #name or //@name.
  • features lists the platform features the script uses by id (such as file.write, scriptui.dialog, menuCommand or e4x), each with a count and its lines.
  • dom.members lists the document-model member chains as the script writes them (app.activeDocument.layers[].name, with [] for an index and () for a call); a variable assigned from a chain is replaced by that chain, and a name written with \u escapes is read as the name it spells. dom.types lists the type names the script compares with .typename or creates with new, apart from the language's, the platform's and the script's own functions.
  • menuCommands.strings lists the string literals the script passes to executeMenuCommand (app.executeMenuCommand('…')), each with a count and its lines, and menuCommands.dynamic counts the calls whose argument isn't a string literal.
  • outside lists what the script does outside the document: filesWritten (with the open modes used), filesOpened (files opened in other apps), network, nativeCode, otherApps (messages to other apps), environment (environment variables), unseenCode (eval, new Function and $.evalFile) and preferences (the reference app's preferences changed).
  • problems lists what would stop the script loading as written (strings, comments, regular expressions, template literals and XML literals that aren't closed, brackets that don't match, syntax newer than ES3 and unknown directives) and notes on its text (bytes that aren't valid UTF-8, mixed line endings, and nesting deeper than the check follows: more than 4,096 open brackets, or XML literals and the code in their braces nested more than 4,096 levels deep).

Each entry of targets, directives, includes and problems has the line it is on (a problem with the whole text has none) and, when it is in an included file, that file's path as file. features, menuCommands.strings and outside count the script and its included files together and list up to 20 of the script's own lines.

With a path and includes: true, the check also reads the files the #include lines name, and the files their own #include lines name. When the script or one of these files assigns a member chain or a File object to a variable, the check treats the variable as holding it in every file. The check looks for a name in the folder of the file that holds the #include line, then in each folder that the last #includepath line before it names, taken from that same folder. It reads only files inside the script's folder: it doesn't follow an absolute name, a name, folder or link that leads out of the script's folder, a name or folder longer than 4,096 bytes, a file outside the automation read root, or the JSINCLUDE environment variable, and it reads each file once. An include's status is checked, missing, notFollowed, cycle (the file it names includes the file the line is in, directly or through other files), notRead (includes weren't read: no includes, no path, or the web app), limit (one of the limits below was reached) or unreadable (a compiled file, or not text), and its path is the file found, relative to the script's folder. The check reads at most 8 MiB of a file and 32 MiB in all, follows 64 included files to a depth of 16 and looks up at most 1,024 paths for them, keeps 1,000 entries in each list, follows at most 1,000 variables that hold member chains and 1,000 that hold File objects (truncated is true when an entry or a variable was left out), and cuts the text it echoes from the script to 120 characters.

File › Scripts › Check Script… picks a .jsx, .jsxinc or .jsxbin file and shows its report, and File › Open and drag-and-drop show the report for those files too; the web app checks the script without its included files. Over the control channel, ui.checkScript {path? | data? | dataBase64?, name?} does the same, and with a path it checks the included files too.

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"script.check","params":{"path":"/Users/me/Scripts/Tidy Layers.jsx","includes":true}}}

vectorcraft-cli run --cmd script.check --params '{"path": "Tidy Layers.jsx"}'

Transparency and opacity masks

Transparency and opacity-mask commands take ids (or id), so they need no selection. Opacity is a percentage (0..100) in every command (transparency.set, object.setProps, appearance.setItem). transparency.info returns the Transparency panel's values, with null where the objects differ. Making a mask from one object gives it an empty mask and enters mask editing: art drawn then becomes the mask, and the mask commands act on the masked object until transparency.stopEditingOpacityMask. Saves and exports never include the editing layer.

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"transparency.set","params":{"ids":[12],"opacity":40,"blend":"Multiply"}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"transparency.makeOpacityMask","params":{"ids":[12,15],"invert":true}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"transparency.setOpacityMask","params":{"id":12,"clip":false}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"transparency.info","params":{"ids":[12,20]}}}

Knockout Group has three states: transparency.set {knockout: "on"|"off"|"neutral"} (true = on, false = neutral, the default). In a knockout group each child hides what it covers of the children below it; neutral groups pass the enclosing group's setting to their children, off groups never knock out. knockoutShape: true makes the object's opacity and opacity mask scale how much it knocks out. transparency.togglePageKnockoutGroup and transparency.togglePageIsolatedBlending ({value?}) treat the whole page as a knockout or isolated group; they are saved with the document and undoable, and transparency.info reports them. PDF and SVG write knockout groups as soft-masked groups with the same look (the PDF export reports a warning).

Groups are not isolated unless Isolate Blending is on: a blend mode inside a group with opacity, a blend mode, an opacity mask, knockout or a clip reaches the art below the group, on screen and in PDF (a knockout group's elements then composite against the art below it). SVG has no non-isolated groups, so there such groups stay isolated.

transparency.viewOpacityMask {on?, id?} is Alt-clicking the mask thumbnail: the canvas shows only the mask of id (default: the mask being edited, else the first selected masked object) as greyscale coverage (white = opaque) and the mask is edited; called again it shows the artwork, still editing. It is view state of the open document: the canvas (and ui.screenshot) shows it, exports never do, and it ends when mask editing ends.

view.transparencyGrid {on?} (View → Show Transparency Grid; default: toggle) is view state of each open document, like the mask view: the grid shows behind the artboards of the documents that turn it on, on the canvas and in ui.screenshot, never in exports. It returns {on}, isn't undoable and doesn't mark the document changed.

view.globalRulers {global?} (View → Rulers → Change to Global / Artboard Rulers, Cmd+Alt+R; default: toggle) is saved with the document and undoable. With artboard rulers (a new document's) the rulers count from the active artboard's top-left corner, or the zero point set for it; with global rulers, from one document zero point. Command coordinates stay absolute either way. document.rulerOrigin {artboard?} returns the zero point the rulers use with that artboard active; document.setRulerOrigin {x, y, artboard?} and document.resetRulerOrigin {artboard?} set or reset the document's (global rulers) or that artboard's (artboard rulers, kept relative to it so it follows the artboard).

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"view.globalRulers","params":{"global":true}}}

view.videoRulers {} (View → Rulers → Show / Hide Video Rulers) is a UI view flag, not saved with the document and not undoable. Show Video Rulers shows the rulers and has them count the active artboard in video pixels from its top-left corner, whatever the ruler origin: a pixel is as many points wide as the artboard's videoRulerPixelAspectRatio (artboard.setProps) and one point tall, so a 720 × 480 pt artboard at 0.9 reads 800 × 480. Hide Video Rulers puts the rulers back in the General unit from the ruler origin; they stay shown. It returns whether video rulers show.

Clipping masks

object.clippingMask.make clips the selected objects by the topmost one, which may be a path, a compound path or a text object (it loses its paint). Compound holes, even-odd fills, glyph outlines and the union of a group's members clip alike on screen, in raster export and in SVG and PDF. object.clippingMask.release turns the clip group into a plain group and keeps the clipping path with whatever paint it has.

A clipping path can be painted after Make (paint.setFill, paint.setStroke, stroke.set with its ids): its fill paints behind the clipped art and its stroke over it, not clipped, on screen and in SVG and PDF.

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"select.set","params":{"ids":[12,15]}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"object.clippingMask.make","params":{}}}

layer.clippingMask.toggle {id?} is the Layers panel's clipping mask button: the top object of the layer id (default: the one highlighted layer or group row, else the one selected group, else the current layer) becomes its clipping path (unpainted, moved to the bottom of the layer, so art added later is clipped too); called again it releases the mask. It returns {clip}, and the Layers panel underlines clipping-path names.

Object selection

select.set {ids} replaces the selection, select.add {ids} adds to it, and select.toggle {id} or select.toggle {ids} toggles each target. Every id must be a non-negative integer naming an existing object. A malformed or unknown id is an error naming that value; the entire selection is left unchanged. All three commands return {count, ids} for the resulting selection, in selection order. An empty array clears the selection for select.set and leaves it alone for Add and Toggle.

select.key {id} makes id the key object that Align aligns to and Distribute Spacing spaces from (object.align without to, object.distributeSpacing with spacing), and select.key {} cancels it. The id must name a selected top-level object. Layers, compound-path members and objects inside another selected object are not top-level objects. Any other id is an error naming that value, and the key is left unchanged. A null id is an error too. A click with the Selection tool on a selected top-level object, while two or more objects are selected or ruler guides are selected with it, runs select.key {id}, and a click on the key object again runs select.key {}. On a single selected object with no ruler guide selected, or on a group selected with only its own members, select.key sets no key. With a key object (or a key guide), object.align moves the other selected ruler guides too: a vertical guide goes to the key's left edge, centre or right edge, a horizontal one to its top, centre or bottom (#2143). Aligning to the selection or the artboard leaves guides where they are. select.key {guide} makes a selected ruler guide (its guide.list index) the key instead, while one or more objects are selected (#2143). object.align then lines the objects up on the guide and the guide stays put: a vertical guide sets where their left edges, centres or right edges go and vertical alignment leaves them where they are, a horizontal guide the other way round. Selected anchor points align to it the same way. object.distribute {spacing} and object.distributeSpacing {spacing} space the objects from it (see Align to Artboard). A click with the Selection tool on a guide selected with objects runs select.key {guide}, and a click on the key guide again runs select.key {}. The key guide goes when it leaves the selection or no object is left selected, and an object key replaces it (and the other way round). It returns {key}, the key object's id or null, with guide, the key guide's index, when a guide is the key. document.inspect (the inspect_document tool) reports the key object as key, null without one, and the key guide as keyGuide. The ui.pointer and pointer_gesture replies list it as key beside selection. The command palette lists select.key as Cancel Key Object and runs it without an id.

edit.clear {ids?} deletes the exact selected objects, including individual compound-path members; their unselected siblings remain in the compound. Supplying ids works without a selection and overrides selected anchors or guides. Every explicit id is validated before any deletion; a malformed or unknown id leaves the document unchanged. An empty ids array does nothing. Layers themselves are kept, and selecting an ancestor together with its descendants deletes that subtree once. Without ids, direct-selected anchors or selected ruler guides retain their usual Clear behavior. Cut still removes the objects it copied, including a whole compound when a member is selected.

object.setProps with visible: false or locked: true deselects every selected object that is hidden or locked after the call: the selected objects it hides or locks, selected objects inside them, and selected objects that were already hidden or locked. The key object is cleared when it is one of them, and edit.undo selects them again with the key object. When the call changes nothing, it records no undo step and still deselects them. Other object.setProps calls leave the selection unchanged.

layer.move, node.move, layer.merge, layer.flatten, layer.collectInNew, dataset.select, dataset.next and dataset.prev also deselect every selected object that is hidden or locked after the call, such as objects moved into a hidden or locked layer or group, or objects that a data set hides. The key object is cleared when it is one of them, and edit.undo selects them again with the key object.

These Select menu commands select only the objects that select.all selects and the objects inside them: every select.same.* and select.object.* command except select.object.slices, select.nextAbove, select.nextBelow and select.recall. They leave out hidden and locked objects with the objects inside them, the objects on hidden, locked or template layers and sublayers and, in isolation mode, everything outside the isolated group or layer. When the isolated group or layer, or a group or layer it is in, is hidden, locked or a template layer, they also leave out everything in it, and select.all, select.allOnArtboard and select.inverse select nothing. select.object.bristleBrushStrokes selects the objects whose topmost stroke has a bristle brush (type bristle in brush.list), whatever the brush is named; a brush of another type with "Bristle" in its name does not count. select.nextAbove and select.nextBelow select the nearest such object above or below the first selected object among the objects beside it in its group or layer, passing over hidden and locked objects and sublayers. When there is no such object, the selection stays as it is.

Global Edit

Select › Start Global Edit is select.globalEdit {appearance?, size?, artboards?, includeCanvas?}. With one path, compound path or symbol instance selected, it finds the objects of the same shape (moved or scaled copies; not rotated ones), among those select.all would take and the objects in their groups, and returns {active: true, count, ids}, the selected object first. appearance also asks for the same fill and stroke, size for the same width and height; artboards is "all" or a range such as "1, 3-5", and includeCanvas: false leaves out the objects on no artboard. Missing params come from select.globalEditOptions (the Global Edit Options, kept for the session; it returns them with count, the objects a start would take now). The selection stays the one object. Until the next select.globalEdit (Stop Global Edit, {active: false}), what an edit of a selected match changed is given to the other matches, in the same undo step, and nothing else. A changed outline or anchors go to each match in proportion to its size. A changed opacity or blending, and each changed setting of a fill or stroke (paint, weight, opacity, blending, effects…), are copied as they are to the match's fill or stroke of the same kind and rank; its other fills and strokes, and their unchanged settings, stay its own (a stroke weight change leaves the fills). A stroke weight set on the selected match is given as it is, and resizing the outline in proportion never scales a match's stroke weight. Hidden or locked matches are left as they are. A fill or stroke added or removed is added or removed on the matches whose fills and strokes lay in the same order; the others, and any other change to the list, take the whole list. Whenever the document is put back (Undo, Redo, a cancelled drag or scrub), the matches it no longer has leave the Global Edit, which ends when fewer than two are left. An edit that would take any match past the canvas fails with the coordinates-out-of-range error and changes nothing. Nothing of it is saved.

Saved selections

Select → Save Selection… keeps the selected objects under a name, in the document: select.save {name?} (default the first free "Selection N"; an existing name is replaced with the current selection; at most 25 per document, names up to 255 characters) → {name}, one undo step. select.savedList lists the names in menu order, and select.recall {name} selects those objects again, except the ones deleted since and the ones hidden, locked or outside the isolated group or layer (see Object selection) → {count}. Update Selection: select.update {} replaces the objects of the saved selection last recalled in the document with the selected objects → {name}, one undo step. The command is enabled while objects are selected and the document holds that saved selection, under its own name or a name select.editSaved gave it; Undo and Redo do not change which saved selection is recalled. Edit Selection… renames and deletes: select.editSaved {name, newName?, delete?}, or several at once as {edits: [{name, newName?, delete?}…]} in one undo step, where every name is the name before the edit and the names must stay unique. In the desktop app the saved selections are listed at the bottom of the Select menu (select.recall1 … select.recall25) and in the Layers panel menu's Save Selection submenu (select.recall {name}). A file's saved selections are checked when it opens: past 25, blank or repeated names, and ids the document doesn't have are dropped.

Saved views

View → New View… keeps a view under a name, in the document: view.saved.new {name?, center: [x, y], zoom, rotation?} (the desktop app fills in its current view; default the first free "View N"; at most 25 per document, names up to 255 characters, each name once) → {name, index}, one undo step. view.saved.list → {views: [{name, center, zoom, rotation}…]} in menu order. Edit Views… renames and deletes: view.saved.edit {name, newName?, delete?}, or several at once as {edits: [{name, newName?, delete?}…]} in one undo step (→ {views}), where every name is the name before the edit and the names must stay unique; it is enabled while the document has a saved view. In the desktop app the views are listed at the bottom of the View menu (view.goto1 … view.goto25); choosing one sets the current window's zoom, centre and rotation. A file's views are checked when it opens: past 25, blank or repeated names, and views without a finite centre and a positive zoom are dropped.

Ruler guides

Ruler guides are numbered in the order they were made. guide.add {vertical, pos, artboard?} makes one (the x of a vertical guide, the y of a horizontal one, in points) → {index}. With artboard (an artboard's index) it is an artboard guide: it runs across that artboard only, moves with it (artboard.move, a pure move in artboard.setProps, artboard.rearrange), scales with it (artboard.setProps {scaleArt}), is copied with it (artboard.duplicate, artboard.move {copy}) and is deleted with it (artboard.delete); without, a canvas guide runs across the whole canvas. guide.list → [{index, vertical, pos, selected, artboard?}…] (artboard only for artboard guides). guide.select {indexes: [index…], toggle?} selects guides on their own (the art is deselected; toggle adds or removes them) → {selected}. guide.move {index, pos} puts one guide somewhere, guide.move {dx?, dy?, copy?} moves the selected ones (vertical guides by dx, horizontal ones by dy; copy leaves them and selects the moved copies), and guide.remove {index?} deletes one guide or the selected ones → {count}; each is one undo step. With guides selected, edit.clear (Delete) deletes them and object.nudge (the arrow keys) nudges them. View › Guides › Lock Guides (view.guides.lock) deselects them and the paths made into guides, in every open document, and refuses these commands until unlocked. While it is on, a command that selects art, such as edit.undo, edit.redo, object.exitIsolation, object.unlockAll, object.showAll or edit.paste, leaves the guides out.

view.guides.release {index?, ids?} (View › Guides › Release Guides) turns guides into paths → {count}. With index it releases that ruler guide, and with ids it releases those guide paths (and the guide paths inside them). Otherwise it releases the selected guide paths and ruler guides, and with no guide selected it releases every guide path. A released ruler guide becomes a straight path with no fill or stroke, at the top of the guide's layer (or where new art goes, for a guide on no layer). An artboard guide becomes a line across its artboard, and a canvas guide becomes a line across the bounds of all the artboards. index and ids are refused while guides are locked. The release is one undo step, and the released paths are selected. With the Selection, Direct Selection or Group Selection tool, a double-click with Cmd/Ctrl and Shift held releases the ruler guide or guide path under the pointer (pointer_gesture with a doubleclick event and mods: {cmd: true, shift: true}), unless guides are hidden or locked.

The Selection, Direct Selection and Group Selection tools pick a guide within the selection tolerance, over the art (an anchor on it comes first with Direct Selection), and drag the selected guides: mods.alt copies them, mods.shift snaps the dragged guide to the ruler's ticks, and otherwise it snaps to whole pixels, the grid or, with Smart Guides, the edges, centres (so the sides' midpoints) and anchors of the art and of the artboards; with Smart Guides off, View › Snap to Point pulls it onto anchors. In the desktop app a guide dragged out of a ruler snaps the same way and is made where it is released over the canvas (with the Artboard tool, as an artboard guide of the active artboard), a guide dropped off the canvas onto its ruler is deleted, and hidden guides (View › Guides › Hide Guides) can't be picked. An artboard guide is picked, drawn and snapped to across its artboard only.

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"guide.add","params":{"vertical":true,"pos":100}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"guide.add","params":{"vertical":false,"pos":200,"artboard":0}}}
{"name":"pointer_gesture","arguments":{"tool":"selection","events":[{"kind":"down","x":100,"y":50},{"kind":"drag","x":140,"y":50},{"kind":"up","x":140,"y":50}]}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"guide.list","params":{}}}

Symbols, brushes, variables and Image Trace

These families are in the live catalog (command_list / vectorcraft://commands); the prompt trace-and-style already walks Image Trace. Short agent entry points:

  • Symbols: symbol.list (each row includes registration 0..8 and exportType: movieClip|graphic), symbol.new {name?, registration?, exportType?, nineSlice?, nineSliceGuides?, ids?}, symbol.options {name?, newName?, registration?, exportType?, nineSlice?, nineSliceGuides?} (nineSlice bool turns 9-slice guides on or off; nineSliceGuides is [left, top, right, bottom], fractions 0..1 of the symbol size; rows of symbol.list carry nineSlice: the guides or null), symbol.place {name?, x?, y?}, symbol.breakLink {ids?}, symbol.replace, symbol.edit, symbol.duplicate, symbol.selectInstances {name}. symbol.new and symbol.duplicate {name, newName?} trim the white space around the name they are given, as symbol.options trims newName; a blank one gives "New Symbol" or " copy".
  • The Brushes panel menu's Select All Unused: brush.unused (a query) lists the brushes no stroke paints with, in library order, and the panel selects them. Strokes in the art (opacity masks included), symbols, graph designs, pattern tiles and graphic styles count, and so do the strokes in the art of a brush that is used. The art of a brush that is not used does not count, so a brush whose own art is the only art that paints with it is listed.
  • Brushes: brush.list, brush.get {name}, brush.apply {name, ids?}, brush.new {type, name?, params?, ids?}, brush.setCurrent {name}, brush.options {name, params?, newName?}, brush.delete {name} | {names}, brush.duplicate {name, newName?} | {names}, brush.freehand, object.expandBrush {ids?}. brush.new, brush.duplicate and brush.options trim the white space around the name they are given. A blank name gives the type's default name ("New Calligraphic Brush" for a calligraphic brush), and a blank newName gives "<name> copy" in brush.duplicate and keeps the name in brush.options. A Hue Shift colorization given to brush.new or brush.options without a key takes the color that the art's fills and strokes use most (on a tie, the one found first), or black when they use no solid color. brush.options changes only the fields of colorization and of an art brush's scale that params gives, so {colorization: {method: "hueShift"}} keeps a Hue Shift brush's key. brush.new and brush.options take a bristle brush name of at most 31 characters: brush.new refuses a longer one, counting the number it adds to a taken name, and brush.options refuses a longer newName. brush.duplicate does not check the length. A bristle brush that already has a longer name keeps it, and brush.options without a new name, or with a newName that only trims the white space around the name, does not refuse. Other brush types take a name of any length.
  • Variables: variable.list, variable.define {name, kind: text|visibility|image|graph}, variable.bind {variable, ids?}, variable.makeTextDynamic, variable.makeVisibilityDynamic, variable.makeImageDynamic, variable.makeGraphDynamic, variable.unbind, variable.rename, variable.delete, variable.highlight (image values are file paths, graph values CSV as graph.setData). variable.lock {locked?} (Lock Variables) locks or unlocks the variables; without locked it toggles, and the lock is saved with the document. While the variables are locked, variable.define, variable.delete, variable.rename, the four variable.make…Dynamic commands and a variable.loadLibrary that would delete or define a variable (below) fail with "variables are locked"; variable.bind, variable.unbind and the dataset.* commands still run (except a dataset.import whose header would define a variable, below), and variable.list reports locked.
  • Data sets: dataset.list, dataset.new {name, values?}, dataset.set {name, values?}, dataset.rename, dataset.delete, dataset.capture, dataset.update, dataset.select {name}, dataset.next / dataset.prev (each dataset.select / next / prev is one undo step over every bound object), dataset.import {path|data|dataBase64|csv} / dataset.export {path?} (CSV: name, then one column per variable). dataset.import also reads tab-separated files, and files whose first header cell isn't name: every header cell is then a variable, and the rows take the first free names Data Set 1, Data Set 2 and so on. An import error about a row's values or name gives the file line the row starts on. A header cell that names no variable defines one, unbound, in the same undo step as the rows. #Shown defines a visibility variable Shown, @Photo defines an image variable Photo, and a cell without a prefix defines a text variable. A prefixed cell whose name is already a variable of the prefix's kind names it; one whose name is a variable of another kind fails the import. A % cell that isn't a variable's exact name (graph data files) is refused. The reply's defined lists the variables the import defined. While the variables are locked, an import whose header would define a variable fails with "variables are locked".
  • Variable libraries: variable.loadLibrary {path|xml|dataBase64} (the Variables panel menu's Load Variable Library…) replaces the document's variables and data sets with a variable library's in one undo step. The library is XML whose variableSets element holds variableSet elements, each with a variables element of variable elements (varName, trait) and a sampleDataSets element of sampleDataSet elements (dataSetName). Every variable of the document is deleted with its bindings and values, and every data set is deleted. Then the library's variables whose trait is textcontent are defined as text variables, unbound. In each data set, the element named after a text variable gives its value: the text of its p elements, one paragraph each. An element without a p element gives no value. Variables of other kinds are left out with their values: the reply lists them in skipped (name, trait), and its warning names each trait with its count. The reply's deleted and deletedDataSets list the variables and data sets the load deleted. While the variables are locked, a library that would delete or define a variable fails with "variables are locked". The reply is {variables, deleted, deletedDataSets, names, skipped, warning?}. A library larger than 8 MiB, one whose elements nest more than 256 deep, and one with more than 256 variables or 1,000 data sets are refused. Its document type declaration may hold an external identifier, comments, processing instructions and up to 64 entity declarations whose values are plain text (no <, & or %). A declaration that holds anything else, and entity references that expand the library past 8 MiB, are refused. So are a variable or data set declared twice and a value for a variable the library doesn't declare.
  • Batch: action.batch {commands, source?: folder|datasets, folder?, destination?: none|saveAndClose|folder, destinationFolder?, overrideOpen?, overrideSave?, overrideExport?, includeSubdirs?, stopOnError?} plays an action on every file in a folder or every data set (the Actions panel menu's Batch…). Override Save and Override Export end the file name of a step with format in that format, in lower case and without a leading dot, when it is one of the format's extensions (.svg and SVG give .svg, .JPEG gives .jpeg), and otherwise in the format's first extension (png8 gives .png, template gives .vctemplate). A step without format keeps the source file's extension (.vectorcraft over data sets).
  • Image Trace: imageTrace.presets, imageTrace.make {id?, preset?, params?, view?} (or imageTrace.makeAndExpand), imageTrace.setView, imageTrace.expand, imageTrace.release. A key inside params that is not one of the trace settings, such as colours, is an error that names it and the nearest setting (colors), as for a command's own keys.
  • Live Paint: livePaint.make, livePaint.merge, livePaint.fill {group?, point}, livePaint.strokeEdge, livePaint.info {group}, livePaint.release, livePaint.expand, and livePaint.gapOptions {ids?, detect?, size?, color?, closeGaps?} (Object › Live Paint › Gap Options). With detect: true, paint stops at the gaps that run from a loose end of a path to the nearest path, up to size wide (small 3 pt, medium 6 pt, large 12 pt, or a number of points up to 72); closeGaps: true adds an unpainted path across each of them. The options are saved with the group, and the reply and livePaint.info list the gaps up to size wide that no path closes, as [x0, y0, x1, y1]. livePaint.merge keeps the bottom group's options when its Gap Detection is on, and otherwise turns Gap Detection on with small when another merged group has it on. When Gap Detection is off in every merged group, it stays off. view.livePaintGaps (View › Show Live Paint Gaps, or Hide Live Paint Gaps while it is on) turns the drawing of those gaps on or off. While it is on and edges show, the canvas draws the gaps of each selected Live Paint group in that group's Gap Preview Color. The reply is the new state (true when on). The Live Paint Selection tool's options (tool.setOption {tool: "livePaintSelection", values}; double-clicking the tool opens them, dialog livePaintSelectionOptions) are selectFills and selectStrokes (both on), color (the highlight color, #rrggbb or a layer color's name; #ff2a2a) and width (the highlight's width, 1 to 20 screen pixels; 4). With selectStrokes off, Alt-click selects the face under the pointer as a click does; with selectFills off, a click selects the edge nearest the pointer as Alt-click does; with both off, a click selects no face or edge. livePaint.merge keeps the bottom Live Paint group's id, name, lock, visibility, transparency, appearance, opacity mask and graphic style link. With a Live Paint group among the objects, livePaint.make adds the others to it as livePaint.merge does, and so does livePaint.fill {ids} when point is outside that group's faces. livePaint.fill does not fill a face that is hidden on its own. With group, a point on a hidden face is an error. With ids, at a point on a hidden face it fills the face there of another Live Paint group among the objects; otherwise it returns an error and does not merge the objects.

brush.selectionArt {ids?|id?} (a query) replies {art}: the object that brush.new given the same ids or id, or the same selection, takes as a new scatter or art brush's art and as a new pattern brush's side tile. Several objects become one group, and art is null when nothing is selected or ids name no object. In the app, the Brushes panel's New Brush button, its menu's New Brush… and art dropped on the panel open New Brush (ui.newBrush {ids?}, dialog newBrush, field type). Scatter, art and pattern need art. OK opens that type's Brush Options for the new brush; for a scatter, art or pattern brush they preview the art from brush.selectionArt. Their OK runs brush.new {type, name, params, ids?}, and Cancel adds no brush.

dataset.select, dataset.next and dataset.prev reply {name, applied, skipped}. They add overflow: [id…] when area type or type on a path whose text they rewrote doesn't fit, as text.setText does (see Fitting area type). They skip an object that is locked or inside a locked layer or group, and count it in skipped. They rewrite hidden objects and objects inside a hidden layer or group, which stay hidden unless one of the data set's visibility values shows them. When such a layer is shown again, its type holds the data set's text. They apply the values in the order the variables are defined. Of two variables of the same kind on one object, the one defined last wins. dataset.list, dataset.capture and dataset.update read hidden objects and objects inside a hidden layer or group as well.

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"symbol.list","params":{}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"brush.list","params":{}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"variable.list","params":{}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"dataset.list","params":{}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"dataset.capture","params":{}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"dataset.select","params":{"name":"Data Set 1"}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"imageTrace.presets","params":{}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"command.batch","params":{"label":"Offset","commands":[{"command":"object.move","params":{"dx":10,"dy":10,"copy":true}}]}}}

When no object is bound to a variable that the data set has a value for, for example after dataset.import defines new variables without binding them, dataset.select, dataset.next and dataset.prev apply nothing and add warning, which the app's status bar shows: "nothing in data set A was applied: no object is bound to a variable it has a value for". A data set with a value for a bound variable gives no warning even when every object bound to it is locked; skipped counts those objects.

dataset.update {all?} writes what the bound objects hold now into the active data set, for each variable that the data set holds. A variable whose objects report no value, because they are locked, inside a locked layer or group, or deleted, keeps the data set's value. With all: true it also adds every other variable that the objects report a value for. The reply gives values, the number of values it wrote, and left, the variables it did not write.

Graphic styles

A graphic style holds an appearance (fills, strokes, effects) plus opacity, blend mode, isolate and knockout. graphicStyle.new {name?, id?} captures an object (a group without its own fills or strokes lends its topmost object's, type its characters'). graphicStyle.apply {name, ids?} gives the objects the style and links them; add: true adds the style on top of the existing appearance instead. Linked objects stay linked while they keep the style's look: editing their appearance or transparency breaks the link, and graphicStyle.redefine {name?, id?} updates only the objects still linked. graphicStyle.list returns each style with the ids linked to it and the style of the first selected object; select.same.graphicStyle selects an object's fellow users. Styles keep placed gradients relative to the bounds of the object they were made from, and each object a style is applied to gets them at the same place relative to its own bounds.

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"graphicStyle.new","params":{"id":12,"name":"Glow"}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"graphicStyle.apply","params":{"name":"Glow","ids":[15,20]}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"graphicStyle.redefine","params":{"id":12}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"graphicStyle.list","params":{}}}

graphicStyle.merge {names, name?} adds a style stacking the fills, strokes and effects of two or more styles (each on top of the ones before it) with the first one's transparency; graphicStyle.move {name, to} reorders the list (what dragging a style in the panel does). Override Character Color (graphicStyle.setOptions {overrideCharColor}, the preference overrideCharColor, on by default) makes a style applied to type replace its characters' fill and stroke with the style's fills and strokes; off, the characters keep their colour under them.

Editing appearance stacks

effect.move {from, to, fromItem?, toItem?, copy?} reorders an effect or moves it between the object's effects (null) and a fill's or stroke's (an item index), as dragging its row in the Appearance panel does; copy: true copies it (Alt-drag). appearance.duplicateItem {index, to?} / {indices} and appearance.removeItem {index | indices} act on several fills/strokes at once, and appearance.showAllHidden makes every hidden fill, stroke and effect visible again. In remote mode, effect.dialog {effect, index, item} opens an applied effect's dialog prefilled (ui.dialog.confirm runs effect.setParams); see the control protocol for the "already applied" question.

Overprint

Each fill and stroke (and each character's fill and stroke) can overprint: its inks print over the inks below instead of knocking them out. object.setOverprint {fill?, stroke?, item?, ids?} sets it (groups set their contents, type its characters too; item aims at one appearance item) and attributes.info {ids?} reads it back (null where the objects differ). Overprint Preview (view.overprintPreview) and Separations Preview show it; rendering approximates it by darkening (the stronger ink of each plate wins, so a colour overprinting itself stays that colour). Older files that listed Overprint Black objects in the document get the flags on load. PDF export writes them with advanced.overprint: "preserve" (the default): each overprinting fill and stroke sets a graphics state with /OP true /op true /OPM 1 (/OPM 0 in PDF/A). With discardWhiteOverprint (document.setup, on by default) white ones knock out instead; "discard" writes none.

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"object.setOverprint","params":{"ids":[12],"stroke":true}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"attributes.info","params":{"ids":[12]}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"view.overprintPreview","params":{"on":true}}}

Revolve (initial 3D effect)

effect.apply {effect: "threeD.revolve", params: {...}, ids?, item?} applies a live Revolve to a path or compound path. effect.dialog opens compact options with artboard rotation and light gizmos, effect.setParams edits it, and effect.expandAppearance bakes it into shaded vector faces. Native saves keep the editable source; SVG/PDF exports bake the same faces the canvas draws. Only one Revolve is supported per profile.

Parameters: angle (degrees, 0..360, default 360), offset (points, 0..100000, default 0), edge ("left" or "right"), rotationX/Y/Z (degrees, defaults 0/0/0), perspectiveAngle (degrees, 0..160, default 0; an earlier perspective, 0..100%, is read as the angle that shows the same view), segments (8..128, default 64), shade (default true), lightColor (opaque #rrggbb, default #ffffff), lightIntensity (0..100%, default 80), lightRotation (degrees, -180..180, default 125.26; 0 on the object's right, counterclockwise), lightHeight (degrees, 0..90, default 30; 90 straight in front), lightBehind (default false), lightSoftness (0..100%, default 0), ambient (Ambient Light, 0..200%, default 25; an earlier lightAzimuth and lightElevation are read as the same light), cap (boolean, default true), and expandVisibleOnly (boolean, default true). A vertical-line profile needs a positive offset to have a radius. cap closes each circular endpoint of an open profile with one curved, uniformly shaded vector path. Body faces share that curved rim. Partial sweeps use sectors and leave their angular cut sides open. Closed or coincident-end profiles are unchanged. Missing cap enables caps; set it to false for open endpoints. Shaded Revolve surfaces automatically interpolate curved-surface lighting with vector gradients while preserving profile corners and flat caps. There is no separate smoothing parameter. shade: false disables all lighting. Visibility of intersecting surfaces is approximate. lights is null by default, retaining the earlier single-light controls. Set an array of one to ten objects to replace that light: {color: "#rrggbb", intensity: 0..100, rotation: -180..180, height: 0..90, softness: 0..100, behind: bool}. Defaults per light are white, 80%, 125.26 degrees, 30 degrees, 0%, false. Omitted fields use defaults; unknown nested keys and malformed lists are refused before mutation. ambient is shared and counted once. Set lights: null to restore the earlier controls. The list is saved in the native file and survives preview, undo and expansion.

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"effect.setParams","params":{"index":0,"item":null,"params":{"lights":[{"color":"#ff8040","rotation":135,"height":35,"intensity":70},{"color":"#4080ff","rotation":-45,"height":20,"intensity":40}],"ambient":20}}}}

lightColor changes only the earlier directional light; ambient stays neutral. Invalid command colors are refused before editing, and invalid loaded colors fall back to white. Colored smooth faces use ordinary RGB vector gradients; source gradients keep their placement and opacity with vector blending/masks. Pattern tiles retain neutral shading. The dialog's Lighting tab manipulates the same lightRotation and lightHeight parameters; its guide visibility and tab choice are UI state, excluded from saved effect parameters. expandVisibleOnly is saved with the effect. It trims covered portions of opaque solid surfaces only when running effect.expandAppearance, preserving visible interiors through openings. Set it to false to expand all faces. Live preview and SVG/PDF baking keep the full surface. Transparent/unknown paint, non-normal blending, later geometry effects or bounded visibility failures preserve all faces. See Revolve for the scope and limitations.

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"effect.apply","params":{"effect":"threeD.revolve","item":null,"params":{"angle":360,"offset":20,"edge":"left","rotationX":0,"rotationY":0,"rotationZ":0}}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"effect.setParams","params":{"index":0,"item":null,"params":{"angle":180,"lightRotation":30,"lightColor":"#ff8040","cap":true}}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"effect.setParams","params":{"index":0,"item":null,"params":{"expandVisibleOnly":true}}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"effect.expandAppearance","params":{}}}

3D lighting presets

effect.lightingPreset {index: int, preset: "standard" | "diffuse" | "topLeft" | "right", item?, ids?, target?} replaces the lighting rig and ambient of an existing Revolve, Extrude & Bevel, Inflate or Rotate effect. item: null addresses object-level effects; an item index addresses a fill/stroke's effects. All targets must have a 3D effect at index, otherwise nothing changes. The operation is one undo step and preserves Shading, geometry, source paint and the view.

The four original rigs use one white front light at 80% intensity. Standard keeps the existing default; Diffuse uses 100% Softness and 35% Ambient Light, Top Left uses Rotation 135 and Height 45, and Right uses Rotation 0 and Height 30. Full values and the public behavior reference are in 3D lighting presets. These are original settings, not imported preset files. The document stores their numeric settings; manual edits remain possible through effect.setParams.

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"effect.lightingPreset","params":{"index":0,"item":null,"preset":"topLeft"}}}

Extrude & Bevel, Inflate and Rotate

effect.apply {effect: "threeD.extrude" | "threeD.inflate" | "threeD.rotate", params: {...}, ids?, item?} applies the other 3D types to a path or compound path, live like Revolve (the source stays editable; SVG/PDF exports and effect.expandAppearance use the same shaded faces). An object takes one 3D effect, Revolve included.

threeD.extrude: depth (points, 0..2000, default 50), twist (degrees, 0..360, 0), taper (1..100%, 100), cap (true solid, false hollow), bevel (none, classic, rounded, cove, step, ogee), bevelWidth and bevelHeight (points, default 4), bevelRepeat (1..32), bevelSpace (0..90%), bevelInside, bevelBothSides. threeD.inflate: depth (points, default 0), twist (degrees, 0..360, 0), taper (1..100%, 100), cap (true solid, false open behind), volume (0..100%, default 50), bothSides. All three take rotationX/Y/Z (degrees, -180..180, default the Off-Axis Front position -21.88/-23.4/9.06), perspectiveAngle and the lighting parameters as Revolve. See three-d.md for the Position presets, the scope and what differs from the public documentation.

Edit Colors and Recolor Artwork

edit.colors.invert, edit.colors.toCMYK, edit.colors.toGrayscale, edit.colors.toRGB, edit.colors.saturate, edit.colors.adjustBalance and recolor.apply recolour everything inside the selection (or ids) in one undo step: fills, strokes, text, gradient stops, gradient-mesh points, embedded images (a recoloured copy of the image; linked images are left alone) and the tiles of pattern fills and strokes (a recoloured copy saved as a new pattern swatch, e.g. "Dots 2"; the original pattern is untouched). includeImages: false / includePatterns: false leave images or patterns out. The Blend commands also grade gradient meshes, which keep their shading.

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"recolor.colors","params":{}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"recolor.apply","params":{"map":{"#ff0000":"#0055ff"},"includeImages":false}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"edit.colors.adjustBalance","params":{"mode":"cmyk","m":-20,"k":10}}}

Recolor Artwork keys colours by identity, not hex: recolor.colors lists each colour's key ("rgb 255 0 0", "cmyk 0 100 100 0", "gray 40", "lab 55 60 -40"; RGB in 0–255 levels, CMYK and Gray in percent), so CMYK, Lab and RGB colours that look alike stay apart, and every colour parameter also accepts a key. recolor.colors and recolor.reduce read the objects in ids, else the selection. With nothing selected they read the objects select.all takes, which leaves out hidden and locked art, and the selection is left unchanged; hidden and locked objects inside a group are left out too, also in isolation mode. recolor.reduce {colors?, method?, preserve?, limitTo?, ids?} groups those colours into rows of similar colours (k-means in Lab, weighted by use; tints of a global swatch share its row; White and Black are preserved by default) and returns a map of rows [{from: [keys], to: key}] to edit and pass to recolor.apply {map, method?, model?, limitTo?, group?, groupColors?, rename?}. For scripts and MCP, model selects the replacement color model: keep (default) retains each original color's model, new takes the replacement's model, and document converts replacements and edited color groups to the document's model. Invalid values fail before editing. This parameter is not a control in the Recolor Artwork dialog. A returned note reports a model conversion in keep mode.

method says how a row's colours take its new colour: exact, preserveTints (tints of the row's darkest colour stay tints), scaleTints (every colour becomes a tint as light, relative to the darkest), tintsShades (lighter and darker than the row's average become tints and shades) or hueShift (the most saturated colour takes the new colour, the others turn by the same hue). limitTo snaps new colours to a swatch library's nearest colour; recolor.randomize {map, order?, saturationBrightness?, seed?} shuffles or varies new colours; a row with exclude: true keeps its colours. swatch.editGroup {group, colors, rename?} rewrites a colour group in one undo step (art linked to its global swatches follows), and recolor.apply with group recolours the art and rewrites the group together. Art linked to one of the group's global swatches stays linked when the swatch is rewritten with its row's new color (the default without groupColors when the rows follow the group's order), so a later swatch.edit still reaches it; the reply's relinked counts the other linked paints that took the group's new colors. Art in a row that is linked to a swatch rewritten with another color keeps its row's new color, even an unchanged one, and is no longer linked to the swatch, as does art whose color the row converts into the art's own color model. Without group, the swatch keeps its color and the recolored art is no longer linked to it.

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"recolor.reduce","params":{"colors":2,"preserve":{"grays":true}}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"recolor.apply","params":{"map":[{"from":["cmyk 0 100 100 0","cmyk 0 40 40 0"],"to":"cmyk 100 50 0 0"}],"method":"scaleTints"}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"swatch.editGroup","params":{"group":"Brights","colors":["#ff0000","cmyk 0 0 100 0"]}}}

Group, layer and type appearance

Groups and layers carry fills, strokes and effects of their own, as in the reference app: their fills and strokes paint every member's geometry, and their effects apply to the members as one piece (one combined drop shadow; a Transform or Warp moves or bends the whole group). Their Contents row (type: Characters) is a slot in the stack: fills and strokes above it paint over the members (characters), those below under them; new items go above it (on an image, below it). appearance.moveItem {"from": "contents", "to": n} puts the row above the bottom n items, and an item move takes contents to say where the row ends up in the same undo step; document.node shows the slot as appearance.contents_index. The appearance.*, effect.* and graphicStyle.apply commands edit the selected objects themselves (a group's own stack; layers through ids) or, with target: "contents", the objects inside the groups and layers. appearance.targetContents selects a group's members (double-clicking the Contents row). SVG and PDF export bake a group's own fills, strokes and geometry effects into paths; its raster effects stay a filter on the whole group in SVG and become an image of the whole group in PDF. A geometry effect on one of type's own fills or strokes (effect.apply with item) reshapes the whole outline that the fill or stroke paints, and SVG, PDF, EPS and the other vector exports write such type as paths. On type that holds inline graphics, those effects are drawn and exported only when the type has a geometry effect or perspective of its own. An image's own fills and strokes draw only when they carry a visible Convert to Shape or Outline Object effect: Convert to Shape sizes its shape from the image's box, Outline Object gives the image's frame, the effects after it reshape that shape, and the effects before it are left out. Its Contents row stands for the image; appearance.addFill and appearance.addStroke put a new item right below that row, so it paints under the image. SVG, PDF, EPS and the other vector exports write those items as paths, and effect.expandAppearance turns them into objects in a group with the image. Use Preview Bounds measures an image by its frame.

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"appearance.addFill","params":{"ids":[7]}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"appearance.moveItem","params":{"ids":[7],"from":"contents","to":1}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"effect.apply","params":{"ids":[2],"effect":"stylize.dropShadow"}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"appearance.addStroke","params":{"ids":[7],"target":"contents"}}}

Swatch libraries

Swatch libraries are read-only sets of swatches computed in code (Web Safe 216, Grays and Neutrals, Earth Tones, Skin Tone Ramps, Pastels, Brights, Metallic Gradients, Perceptual Scales, Harmony Sets). swatch.library.list lists them (id, name, category, count) and swatch.library.get {library} returns one's swatches and colour groups in the swatch.list shape (library is an id or a name). swatch.library.add {library, names?} copies swatches into the document as one undo step: a colour group's name brings the whole group, a swatch's name the swatch alone, no names the whole library; swatches the document already has (same name and paint) are reported under existing and not added again. apply: "fill"|"stroke" also applies the first one, in the same undo step (what clicking a swatch in the library panel does). swatch.resetDefaults {replace?} brings back the missing default swatches.

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"swatch.library.get","params":{"library":"earth-tones"}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"swatch.library.add","params":{"library":"earth-tones","names":["Clay"]}}}

swatch.library.save {path?, format?: "vcswatches"|"gpl"|"ase"|"css", names?, name?, user?} writes the document's swatches as a library (.vcswatches keeps colour models, global, spot, gradients and colour groups; .gpl is 8-bit RGB; a swatch exchange .ase file keeps solid colors in their own model (RGB, CMYK, Lab or Gray) as global, spot or process colors, and color groups, writes a tint swatch as the color it shows and leaves gradients out; CSS writes custom properties); without path it returns {data}, or {dataBase64} for .ase, and user: true saves into the user library folder of the desktop app (listed as category user, User Defined). The reply's count is the number of swatches written: .gpl and .ase hold solid colors only, and CSS leaves freeform gradients out. swatch.library.load {path? | data? | dataBase64?, name?} loads a .vcswatches, .gpl, swatch exchange (.ase) or color book (.acb) file, or another document's swatches, as a library to add from. From an .ase file it reads RGB, CMYK, Lab and Gray colors as global, spot or process swatches and keeps their color groups. A color book (such as a Pantone book you own) gives its RGB, CMYK or Lab colors named with the book's prefix and suffix ("PANTONE 185 C"), as spot colors unless the book marks them as process colors; .acb files are read, never written. A file in the user library folder, or a file with the same extension and bytes as one there, loads as that User Defined library (category user). swatch.library.copyToUser {library} copies a loaded library into the user library folder of the desktop app (a library file as it is; a document's swatches or a library loaded from data or dataBase64 as .vcswatches) under a name no file there has, and lists it as User Defined from then on; until the app quits, commands given its loaded id use the copy → {library, name, count, path, copied} (copied: false when the folder already held the same file). Without a user library folder the command is disabled.

Graphic style libraries

Graphic style libraries are read-only sets of graphic styles generated in code (Shadows and Glows, Outlines and Rules, Hand-Drawn, Gradient Finishes, Shape Effects, Blends and Transparency). graphicStyle.libraries lists them (id, name, category: builtIn, user or loaded, count) and graphicStyle.library {library} returns one's styles in the graphicStyle.list shape (library is an id or a name). graphicStyle.addFromLibrary {library, name? | names?} copies styles into the document's Graphic Styles as one undo step (no names: the whole library); styles the document already has (same name and look) are reported under existing, a name another look has gets a number, and the patterns the styles paint with come along. apply: true also applies the first one to ids or the selection (add: true adds its appearance on top instead), in the same undo step: what clicking a style in the library panel does. Library styles keep placed gradients in unit-box space, so each object gets them at the same place relative to its bounds.

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"graphicStyle.library","params":{"library":"hand-drawn"}}}
{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"graphicStyle.addFromLibrary","params":{"library":"hand-drawn","name":"Crosshatch","apply":true}}}

graphicStyle.saveLibrary {path?, names?, name?, user?} writes the document's styles as a .vcstyles library (JSON: the styles unlinked from swatches, with their opacity, blend mode, isolate and knockout, and the patterns they paint with); without path it returns {data}, and user: true saves into the user library folder of the desktop app (category user, User Defined). graphicStyle.loadLibrary {path? | data? | dataBase64?, name?} loads a .vcstyles file, or another document's graphic styles, as a library to add from; a file in the user library folder, or a file with the same extension and bytes as one there, loads as that User Defined library.

Symbol libraries

Symbol libraries are read-only sets of symbols: one generated in code (Basic Shapes), the .vectorcraft files in the user library folder of the desktop app (User Defined) and the symbols of any document loaded with symbol.loadLibrary. symbol.libraries lists them (id, name, category: builtIn, user or loaded, count) and symbol.library {library} returns one's symbols (name, size, registration, exportType; library is an id or a name). symbol.addFromLibrary {library, name? | names?} copies symbols into the document's Symbols as one undo step (no names: the whole library), with their Symbol Options and the resources they use: the symbols they nest, patterns, swatches (swatchConflict as for edit.paste), brushes, graphic styles and images. Symbols the document already has (same name and art) are reported under existing; a name another symbol has gets a number, and names gives each one's name in the document. The first one becomes the current symbol. place: true also places an instance of it with its registration point at x, y (default: the first artboard's centre) in the same step: what dragging a symbol out of the library panel onto the canvas does.

{"name":"run_command","arguments":{"command":"symbol.addFromLibrary","params":{"library":"basic-shapes","name":"Star","place":true,"x":120,"y":80}}}
